. Thus, we have that in the formula (
2.5
) for
F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))
, the addends
\xi\in V
yielding a positive contribution satisfy
\xi_{3}\neq 0
.
Without restriction we can consider the case
\xi_{3}>0
. Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))
and
\xi\in V
be such that
j+\varepsilon\xi\notin E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
, hence,

|(j+\varepsilon\xi)_{3}-i_{3}|=\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}
. Let now
k\in E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
be the point with maximal
e_{3}
-coordinate among all other points in
E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
in the same
e_{3}
-column as
j
, i.e.,
k_{1}=j_{1},k_{2}=j_{2}
, and
m_{3}\leq k_{3}\leq j_{3}
for all
m:=(j_{1},j_{2},m_{3})\in E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
. Now either
k=j
, in which case we have that
k+\varepsilon\xi\notin E
since
E\subset E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Otherwise, if
k\neq j
, then
-\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}\leq(k+\varepsilon\xi)_{3}-i_{3}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}-\varepsilon
. Therefore, due to the choice of
k
, we again have that
k+\varepsilon\xi\notin E
. Note that in the previous procedure, since
\|\xi\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, for each
\xi\in V
we have that each
k\in E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
(with
k+\varepsilon\xi\notin E
) is chosen at most once for a point
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\cap E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
with
j+\varepsilon\xi\notin E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
. Therefore, in (
2.5
), summing over all
j
for
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
and the corresponding
k
for
E
, we obtain (
3.17
).

Step 1.3
.(Conclusion) Combining now (
3.13
) and (
3.17
), and recalling the notation in (
3.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))+\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E,Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))+\omega(\varepsilon)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))

\displaystyle\leq F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)+C\omega(\varepsilon)F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,,

where we used (
1.12
) and (
3.8
). This finishes the proof of (
3.9
).

Step 2
.(Curvature Estimate) Next, we prove (
3.10
). This follows from the property that for every
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
we have

(3.18)

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\leq CF_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\,,

for an absolute constant
C>0
. Indeed, once the above inequality is shown, by summing over all
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, (
3.10
) follows from (
1.12
), (
3.8
), and (
3.18
).

We now show (
3.18
). To this end, let
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and assume without loss of generality that
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
is not flat in
Q_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, since otherwise the statement is trivial by (
2.7
) and assumption (i) on
W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}
. Since
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
is cubic in
Q_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, this also implies that
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
is not locally flat in
Q_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, see Definition
2.4
. We can also assume that
E
is not locally flat in
Q_{\frac{5}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, since otherwise
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
would be flat in
Q_{\frac{5}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, and again the statement would be trivial.
Consequently, as
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
is not locally flat in
Q_{\frac{5}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, we have that, up to rotation, there exists
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\nu_{i}\in\{0,1\}
for all
i=1,2,3
, such that
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\cap Q_{2\varepsilon}(i+\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}\nu)

does not satisfy any of the conditions (i)-(iii) of Definition
3
. Moreover, since
E
is not locally flat in
Q_{\frac{5}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, there exists
Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,2}
such that
Q_{\frac{5}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\cap Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\neq\emptyset
, but in
Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})
the set
E
does not satisfy any of the conditions (i)-(iii) of Definition
3
. We can now proceed similarly to Step 1.1: for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we have that
Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\subset Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}}(j)\subset Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and therefore, due to property (ii) below (
2.7
) (see also Remark
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(j,E)\geq c\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta^{-1-q}_{\varepsilon}\,.

As before in Step 1.1, along with the fact that
\#\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))\geq C\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}}
, this yields

(3.19)

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\geq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))}\varepsilon^{3}W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(j,E)\geq c\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2-q}\,.

On the other hand, due to (
3.7
), we have that
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
is cubic in
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(j)
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))
, and therefore by property (iii) below (
2.7
) and the fact that
\#\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))=\eta^{3}_{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon^{3}
, we have

(3.20)

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}},Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))}\varepsilon^{3}W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(j,E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}})\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta^{2-q}_{\varepsilon}\,.

Hence, (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) imply (
3.18
). This concludes Step 2.

Step 3
.(Cardinality of the difference) The goal of this final step is to prove (
3.11
). Recall the notation introduced in (
3.12
). Due to (
1.12
), (
2.8
), (
3.8
), and (
3.15
), for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
we have

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip-10.00002pt\#\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}&\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\eta^{-2+q}_{\varepsilon}\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\\
&\leq C\eta^{-2}_{\varepsilon}\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,.\end{split}

Now, let
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}^{\mathrm{good}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be such that
(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
, i.e.,
E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}(i)\neq\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))
and also
E\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\neq\emptyset
. Then, since
E
is a simple coordinate laminate in
Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
, assuming again without loss of generality that
\nu=e_{3}
, we can find
k\in E\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}(i)
such that
k+\varepsilon e_{3}\notin E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
or
k-\varepsilon e_{3}\notin E
. By the laminate structure of
E
, the same holds true for all
m\in\{k+(\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\{0\})\}\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
. Thus,

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E,Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\geq c_{e_{3}}\varepsilon^{2}(\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\varepsilon^{2})=c_{e_{3}}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\,,

and therefore, for a constant
C>0
that additionally depends on
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
,

(3.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\#\big\{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}^{\mathrm{good}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\neq\emptyset\big\}&\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sum_{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}^{\mathrm{good}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\\
&\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)\,,\end{split}

where we again used (
3.8
).
Since on each cube we have that
\#((E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\leq\eta^{3}_{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon^{3}
, by (
3.21
) and (
3.22
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\#((E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\left(\#\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}^{\mathrm{bad}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\#\big\{Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}^{\mathrm{good}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\neq\emptyset\big\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{-3}\left(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\right)\,.

This proves (
3.11
) and concludes the proof of the lemma.

∎

For a set
\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
of finite perimeter, we will also denote by

\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},A):=\int_{\partial^{*}\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap A}\varphi(\nu_{\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\,

its anistropic perimeter in
A\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with respect to the density
\varphi
defined in
(
2.10
)
. Again, if
A=U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we omit the dependence on
A
in the notation. Moreover, for a regular set
\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{reg}}(U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, we denote by
\bm{A}
the second fundamental form of
\partial\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, i.e.,
|\bm{A}|=\sqrt{\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2}}
, where
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are the principal curvatures of
\partial\hat{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. As a second preliminary step, we replace the set
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
by a regular set, whose perimeter and curvature energy can be controlled by the discrete perimeter and curvature energy of
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
, respectively. This is formulated, again in a local fashion, in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.5

(Smoothening of cubic sets)
.

There exist constants
C=C(\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V})>0
,
c_{0}>0
, and for every
E\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
there exists a set
\hat{E}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{reg}}(U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with
Q_{\varepsilon}(E)\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\hat{E}\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
1.9
)), so that the following holds true. For every open subset
\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with Lipschitz boundary, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\in(0,1)
, such that:

(i)

For the anisotropic perimeter of
\hat{E}
with density
\varphi
, we have

(3.23)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)+\omega(\varepsilon)F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,,

where
\omega(\varepsilon)\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

(ii)

For the curvature energy of
\hat{E}
, we have

(3.24)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq CF_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(iii)

For the difference in volumes, we have

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}((\hat{E}\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E))\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}\left(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\right)\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(iv)

We have

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\hat{E},Q_{\varepsilon}(E)\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\geq c_{0}\eta_{\varepsilon}\,.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps. In the first step, we explain how to reduce the problem to the case that
E
is an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale set in the sense of (
3.7
). In Step 2 we show how to construct the set
\hat{E}
for
\eta_{\varepsilon}=1
. In Step 3 we discuss the shape of the interface of the smooth replacement set and derive the estimates in the case that
\eta_{\varepsilon}=1
. In Step 4 we perform a scaling argument and derive the corresponding estimates for general
\eta_{\varepsilon}>0
, which allows us to conclude.

Step 1
.(Reduction to
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale sets)
First of all, without loss of generality we can assume that
E
is an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale set in the sense of (
3.7
). To this end, let
U^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be an open Lipschitz set such that
\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\subset U^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}>0
be small enough such that Lemma
3.4
is applicable for
U^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
in place of
U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. We apply Lemma
3.4
for
U^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
to obtain
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
such that
E\subset E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
and (
3.9
)–(
3.11
) hold true for
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
. We perform the construction presented in Steps 2–4 below for
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
in place of
E
and for
U^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
in place of
U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
to get (
3.23
)–(
3.26
) for
E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
in place of
E
. Then, we see that (
3.23
)–(
3.26
) also hold for
E
due to (
3.9
)–(
3.11
),
E\subset E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}
, and
\mathcal{L}^{3}((Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}})\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E))\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\varepsilon^{3}\#((E_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\setminus E)\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. Therefore, from now on we can assume that
E
is an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale set.

Step 2
.(Construction of the set at scale
\eta_{\varepsilon}=1
) Let
E\subset\mathbb{Z}^{3}
be a finite set and (according to the notation we introduced in (
1.9
) and the comments thereafter) let
Q(E):=\bigcup_{i\in E}Q(i)\,.

Let
(\zeta_{\sigma})_{\sigma>0}
be a family of smooth symmetric mollifiers in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, i.e., there exists a radially symmetric function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3};[0,+\infty))
such that

(3.27)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\zeta(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1\,,\quad\mathrm{supp}\,\zeta\subset\subset B_{1}\,,\quad\zeta_{\sigma}(x)=\sigma^{-3}\zeta(\sigma^{-1}x)\,.

For every
\sigma>0
consider the function
f_{\sigma}(x):=\left(\chi_{Q(E)}\ast\zeta_{\sigma}\right)(x)
and for
t\in(0,1)
consider the super-level set
E_{\sigma,t}
defined as
E_{\sigma,t}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\colon f_{\sigma}(x)>t\}
. (Here, for notational convenience, we have suppressed the dependence of
f_{\sigma}
on
E
.) Our goal is to choose
c_{0}>0
,
\sigma_{0}>0
, and
t_{0}\in(0,1)
independently of
E
such that

(i)

\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
is a regular
2
-dimensional manifold;

(ii)

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathcal{H}}(E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}},Q(E))\leq\sigma_{0}
;

(iii)

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}}\mathrm{dist}(x,Q(E))\geq c_{0}
.

To see these properties, we define the
N_{0}:=2^{27}
different sets
(E^{k})_{k=1,\ldots,N_{0}}
by
E^{k}:=\bigcup_{j\in I_{k}}Q(j)
,
I_{k}\subset\{-1,0,1\}^{3}
. We note that for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
we find
k
such that

(3.28)

\displaystyle Q(E)\cap Q_{3}(i)=E^{k}+i\,.

In other words, there are only
N_{0}
different possibilities for the shape of
Q(E)\cap Q_{3}(i)
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. For
\sigma_{0}>0
small enough, by (
3.28
), we have that
f_{\sigma_{0}}^{k}(x):=(\chi_{E^{k}}\ast\zeta_{\sigma_{0}})(x)=(\chi_{Q(E)}\ast\zeta_{\sigma_{0}})(x)
for all
x\in Q_{\frac{3}{2}}(i)
. Now let
\mathcal{N}_{k}
be the set of critical values of
f_{\sigma_{0}}^{k}
, which by the Morse-Sard Lemma (cf. Lemma 13.15 in
[
40
]
) is a set of
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-measure zero. In particular, for
\mathcal{N}:=\bigcup_{k=1}^{N_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{k}
we have that
\mathcal{L}^{1}(\mathcal{N})=0
. Thus, we can choose
t_{0}\in(0,1)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathcal{N}
such that (i) is satisfied. In order to obtain (iii), due to (
3.28
), it suffices to choose
t_{0}
and
c_{0}>0
small enough depending on
\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}
such that (iii) is satisfied for all
E^{k}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,N_{0}\}
, in place of
E
. Then, as
E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\supset Q(E)
, (ii) follows from the fact that
\mathrm{supp}\,\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}\subset\subset B_{\sigma_{0}}
.

Step 3
.(Surface energy estimate in the case
\eta_{\varepsilon}=1
)
Similarly to the proof of Lemma
3.4
, we will consider the two families of good and bad cubes with respect to
E
, namely

(3.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}=\left\{Q(i)\colon i\in E,\ E\ \text{simple coordinate laminate in }Q_{3}(i)\right\}\,,\ \mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E}=\{Q(i)\colon i\in E\}\setminus\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}\,.

Let us denote by

(3.30)

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{Q}:=\big\{Q(i)\colon i\in E,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial Q(i)\cap\partial{Q(E)})>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\}

the collection of boundary cubes. For a cube
Q:=Q(i)\in\partial\mathcal{Q}
, let us denote by
\mathcal{T}_{Q}
the (nonempty) collection of those faces of
Q
that also belong to
\partial Q(E)
. For each
T\in\mathcal{T}_{Q}
, let
\nu_{T}\in\{\pm e_{i}\}_{i=1,2,3}
be the constant outward pointing unit normal to
\partial Q(E)
at the face
T
. For the set
E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
we denote its outward unit normal at a point
x\in\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
by
\nu_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}(x)
.

Step 3.1
.(Good cubes) Let
Q=Q(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}
. Fix a boundary face
T\in\mathcal{T}_{Q}
. Provided that
\sigma_{0}>0
is small enough, we can check that
E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
is also a coordinate laminate in
(T)_{2\sigma_{0}}
, (cf. (
1.5
)), in the sense that
\nu_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}(x)=\nu_{T}
for every
x\in\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap(T)_{2\sigma_{0}}
. Indeed, suppose without restriction (after possibly a rotation by a multiple of
\tfrac{\pi}{2}
with respect to a coordinate axis and a reflection) that
\nu_{T}=e_{3}
. Then, since
\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}=\{f_{\sigma_{0}}=t_{0}\}
and
\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}
is nowhere vanishing on
\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
(this is again a consequence of the choice of
\sigma_{0}
and the Morse-Sard Lemma), for every
x\in\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap(T)_{2\sigma_{0}}
and for
l=1,2,3
, we have

(3.31)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nu_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}(x)\cdot e_{l}&=-\frac{\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}}{|\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}|}(x)\cdot e_{l}=-\frac{1}{|\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}(x)|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi_{Q(E)}(y)\nabla\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}(x-y)\cdot e_{l}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&=-\frac{1}{|\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}(x)|}\int_{{Q(E)}\cap B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)}\partial_{l}\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&=\frac{1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{|\nabla f_{\sigma_{0}}(x)|}\int_{\partial(Q(E)\cap B_{\sigma_{0}}(x))}\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}(x-y)\nu_{l}(y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}(y)\,,\end{split}

where
\nu_{l}
denotes the
l
-th component of the outer unit normal to
\partial(Q(E)\cap B_{\sigma_{0}}(x))
, which satisfies
\partial(Q(E)\cap B_{\sigma_{0}}(x))=(\partial B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)\cap\overline{Q(E)})\cup(\partial(Q(E))\cap\overline{B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)})
. Due to (
3.27
), we have
\zeta_{\sigma_{0}}(x-y)=0
on
\partial B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)
. On the other hand, as a consequence of the laminate structure of
E
in
Q_{3}(i)
, for
\sigma_{0}>0
small enough, we get that
\partial(Q(E))\cap\overline{B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)}
is either empty or is contained in the union of
T
and the other 8 faces with outer normal
e_{3}
of neighboring cubes to
Q(i)
that share either an edge or a vertex with
T
. (Note that these neighboring cubes also belong to
Q(E)
.) Therefore, we have that
\nu_{l}=\delta_{3l}
on
\partial(Q(E))\cap\overline{B_{\sigma_{0}}(x)}
, where
\delta_{3l}
denotes the Kronecker delta. In view of (
3.31
), this implies

(3.32)

\displaystyle\nu_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}(x)=\nu_{T}=e_{3}\,.

For good boundary cubes
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{\mathrm{good}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, the laminate structure implies that
\#\mathcal{T}_{Q}\in\{1,2\}
and if
\#\mathcal{T}_{Q}=2
, then two opposite faces are in
\mathcal{T}_{Q}
. Therefore, the outer unit normal vector to
\partial Q(E)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
of boundary faces is well-defined up to sign, and denoted by
\nu_{Q}
. We define

(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}:=\{x+s\nu_{Q}\colon x\in Q,-2\sigma_{0}\leq s\leq 2\sigma_{0}\}\,.

Due to (
3.32
), the fact that
\nu_{T}=\pm\nu_{Q}
, and property (ii) established in Step 2, we have

\displaystyle Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{\mathrm{good}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\implies\quad\partial(E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}})\cap(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}=\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{Q}}(T+s_{T}\nu_{Q})\text{ for some }-\sigma_{0}<s_{T}<\sigma_{0}\,.

Hence, we obtain

(3.33)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}},(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}})=\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(Q(E),(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}})\,,

while, by the flat laminate structure of
E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
in
(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}
, it is clear that

(3.34)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=0\,.

Finally, by the choice of
\sigma_{0}>0
and the definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}
, see (
3.29
), we have

(3.35)

\displaystyle(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}\cap(Q^{\prime})_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}=\emptyset\,\text{ for all }Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}\,.

Step 3.2
.(Bad cubes) Let
Q=Q(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E}
. As noted in (
3.28
), there are at most
N_{0}
different possibilities for the local shape of
E
. In particular, there exists a universal constant
C>0
(only depending on
\sigma_{0},t_{0}
, and these
N_{0}
different configurations
(E^{k})_{k=1,\ldots,N_{0}}
) so that for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E}
,

(3.36)

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}}(i))\leq C\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\int_{\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}}(i)}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq C\,.

We also note that
\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
is covered by the union of
\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}
,
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, together with
\partial E_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\frac{3}{2}}(i)
,
Q(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E}
.

Step 4
.(
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale construction) We now use the previous steps in order to construct a regular set
\hat{E}
from a set
E\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
. By Step 1, we can assume that
E
is an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale set in the sense of (
3.7
).
Now, let
E_{1}:=\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, which is a finite subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. We then choose
(E_{1})_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}
as constructed in Step 2 and define
\hat{E}
by
\hat{E}:=\eta_{\varepsilon}(E_{1})_{\sigma_{0},t_{0}}\cap U
. By standard properties of scaling of sets, we have
Q_{\varepsilon}(E)=\eta_{\varepsilon}Q(E_{1})
(recall (
1.9
)), as well as

(i)

\partial\hat{E}\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is a regular
2
-dimensional manifold;

(ii)

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathcal{H}}(\hat{E},Q_{\varepsilon}(E))\leq\sigma_{0}\eta_{\varepsilon}
;

(iii)

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\hat{E}}\mathrm{dist}(x,Q_{\varepsilon}(E))\geq c_{0}\eta_{\varepsilon}
.

The last property directly implies (
3.26
), and we also get
Q_{\varepsilon}(E)\cap U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\hat{E}\subset U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Now, denote by

(3.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}&:=\{Q\colon Q=Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i),i\in\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{3},\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}Q\in\partial\mathcal{Q}\}\,;\\
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&:=\{Q\colon Q=Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i),i\in\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{3},\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E_{1}},Q\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}\,;\\
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&:=\{Q\colon Q=Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i),i\in\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{3},\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E_{1}},Q\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}\,,\\
\end{split}

where we have used the same notation as in (
3.29
), (
3.30
) for the families
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{E_{1}},\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{E_{1}}
,
\partial\mathcal{Q}
(with respect to
E_{1}
), and for notational convenience we have suppressed the dependence on the original set
E
in the notation for the families
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
By (
3.33
), (
3.34
), and (
3.35
), we have

(3.38)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E},(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}})=\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(Q_{\varepsilon}(E),(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}),\ \ \ \int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=0

for all
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, where we set
(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}:=\eta_{\varepsilon}(\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}Q)_{2\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}
, and

(3.39)

\displaystyle(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}\cap(Q^{\prime})_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}=\emptyset\,\text{ for all }Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\,.

On the other hand, due to (
3.36
) and elementary scaling properties of
\mathcal{H}^{2}
and the second fundamental form, for
Q:=Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we have

(3.40)

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2-q}\,.

Finally, by the simple observation we made at the end of Step 3 and by scaling, we get

(3.41)

\displaystyle\partial\hat{E}\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}}(\partial\hat{E}\cap(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}})\cup\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}(\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}})\,.

Step 4.1
.(Proof of (
3.23
)) By (
3.41
) we can estimate

(3.42)

\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\,.

We start with the good cubes. We use the same notation
\mathcal{T}_{Q}
and
\nu_{T}
as before in Step 3.1. Using (
2.5
), (
2.10
), (
3.38
), and (
3.39
), we have

(3.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}&=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\varepsilon}(E)\cap(Q)_{2\eta_{\varepsilon}\sigma_{0}}^{\mathrm{vert}}}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\\
&=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{good}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{Q}}\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\underset{\xi\cdot\nu_{T}>0}{\sum_{\xi\in V}}\varepsilon^{2}c_{\xi}\leq F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)\,,\end{split}

where we also used (
3.8
) and the fact that
\eta_{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon\in\mathbb{N}
, see (
2.6
). Now, let
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}}
. By the negation of Lemma
3.2
there exists
k\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}
such that for
Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,2}
,

Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\cap Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\neq\emptyset

and

E\cap Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})
does not satisfy any of the properties (i)-(iii) of Definition
3
. By (
2.6
), we actually have that
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big(Q_{\frac{7}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\big)
, provided that we take
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough.

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.4
, for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k)\big)
we have that
Q_{2\varepsilon}(\hat{k})\subset Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{2}}(j)
and therefore, due to property (ii) below (
2.7
), we get

\displaystyle W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(j,E)\geq c\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1-q}\,.

Notice that, since
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k)\big)
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big(Q_{\frac{7}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\big)
, we get that
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big(Q_{4\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\big)
. This, together with the fact that
\#\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))\geq C\frac{\eta^{3}_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{3}}
for an absolute constant
C>0
, leads to

(3.44)

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}\big(E,Q_{4\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))}\varepsilon^{3}W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(j,E)\geq\#\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\frac{\eta_{\varepsilon}}{4}}(k))\,\varepsilon^{3}\cdot c\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1-q}\geq c\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2-q}\,.

In view of (
3.40
) and (
3.44
), we can simply estimate

(3.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}&\leq\varphi_{\mathrm{max}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}})\\
&\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\,\#\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-2+q}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{4\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\\
&\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{q}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,,\end{split}

where we again used (
1.12
) and (
3.8
), and for brevity we set
\varphi_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\varphi
. Here,
C:=C(\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V})>0
, since
\varphi
depends on
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
. Now, (
3.42
), (
3.43
), and (
3.45
) imply (
3.23
) for
\omega(\varepsilon):=C\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{q}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
(see (
2.8
)).

Step 4.2
.(Proof of (
3.24
)) By using (
3.38
), (
3.40
), (
3.41
), and (
3.44
) we can estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma_{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}&\leq\gamma_{\varepsilon}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\int_{\partial\hat{E}\cap Q_{\frac{3}{2}\eta_{\varepsilon}}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2-q}\#\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\\
&\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2-q}\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-2+q}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad}}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E,Q_{4\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\leq CF_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E)\,,\end{split}

which proves (
3.24
), where the last step follows as in (
3.45
).

Step 4.3
.(Proof of (
3.25
)) By the construction of the set
\hat{E}
we have that

\displaystyle\left(\hat{E}\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E)\right)\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\bigcup_{Q\in\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}}\,,

where we recall the notation in (
3.37
), and set
\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:=\{Q\in\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}\colon Q\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}
.
By the fact that we have reduced to the case that
E
is an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale set in the sense of (
3.7
), we have
F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}})\geq c\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}
for all
Q\in\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
for some constant
c>0
depending on
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
. Therefore, by (
1.12
) and (
3.8
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}\big((\hat{E}\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E))\cap\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\leq C\eta^{3}_{\varepsilon}\,\#\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{-1}\eta_{\varepsilon}\sum_{Q\in\partial\mathcal{Q}_{\eta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E,Q_{3\eta_{\varepsilon}})\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E)\,,

where
C>0
only depends on
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
. This finishes the proof of (
3.25
).

∎

We now have all the necessary ingredients to prove the first part of our compactness theorem. The second part on the validity of the
Cauchy-Born rule for discrete symmetric gradients
, i.e., (
2.16
), will be postponed to the next section.

Proof of Theorem
2.8

(i)
.

Having fixed the boundary condition
u_{0}\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let us consider
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
,
E_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
and
u_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}(u_{0},\partial_{D}\Omega,E_{\varepsilon})
, such that

(3.46)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})<+\infty\,.

In particular,
u_{\varepsilon}=u_{0}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(U\setminus\Omega)
(cf. Definition
2.1
).
Consider also a sequence of smooth open sets
(U_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
, with
U_{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\subset U
and

(3.47)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathcal{L}^{3}(U\setminus U_{m})=0,\quad\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial U_{m})<+\infty\,.

Let
\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{reg}}(U)
be the smooth set obtained from
E_{\varepsilon}
through Lemma
3.5
, applied for
(U,U_{m})
in the place of
(U,\widetilde{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
here. This lemma guarantees that
Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap U\subset\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\subset U
, and that we can find a decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\varepsilon_{m}:=\varepsilon(U_{m},U)\in(0,1)
such that for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{m}]
,

(3.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rm{(i)}&\quad\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U_{m})\leq F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})+\omega(\varepsilon)F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,\\
\rm{(ii)}&\quad\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\cap U_{m}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\\
\rm{(iii)}&\quad\mathcal{L}^{3}\big((\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}))\cap U_{m}\big)\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}\left(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,\\
\rm{(iv)}&\quad\dist(U_{m}\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap U)\geq c_{0}\eta_{\varepsilon}\,,\end{split}

where
\omega(\varepsilon)\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}:=\gamma_{\varepsilon}/C
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
, depending only on the coefficients
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
. We also recall that
F_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{per}}(E)\,,F_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{curv}}(E)
are given by (
2.5
) and (
2.7
) respectively.

By
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
we denote the piecewise affine interpolation of
u_{\varepsilon}
with respect to a standard triangulation subordinate to the lattice
\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, see also Subsection 3.2 in
[
44
]
for more details on the definition of the interpolation. By construction, it is clear that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|_{U\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}}\in H^{1}(U\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon};\mathbb{R}^{3})
. By (
1.10
), (
2.6
), and (
3.48
)(iv) we find that
Q\subset U\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})
for every
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,U_{m}\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}}
. Moreover, for each such
Q
, we get

\fint_{Q}{\rm dist}^{2}({\rm Id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon},SO(3))\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C{\rm dist}^{2}(Z+\delta_{\varepsilon}\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|_{Q},\bar{SO}(3))

due to
[
44
, Lemma 3.6(i)]
. Thus, by our structural assumptions on the discrete elastic energy (cf. Subsection
2.2
), which are the same ones as in
[
44
]
, by
[
44
, Lemma 3.7]
, as well as (
3.46
) and (
3.48
), for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
we deduce that

(3.49)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{m}]}\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\int_{U_{m}\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\mathrm{Id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon},SO(3))+\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U_{m})+\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\cap U_{m}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\right)\leq{C}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
m
.

Regarding the boundary conditions, we observe the following: let
\widetilde{u}_{0,\varepsilon}
be the piecewise affine interpolation of the boundary datum
u_{0}\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3})
with respect to the same triangulation of the lattice
\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. Then
\widetilde{u}_{0,\varepsilon}\to u_{0}
locally uniformly on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. The fact that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\equiv\widetilde{u}_{0,\varepsilon}\text{ on }\{x\in U\setminus\Omega\colon\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial(U\setminus\Omega))\geq\sqrt{3}\varepsilon\}\,,
along with (
2.8
), that
\eta_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
[
27
, Remark 3.10]
, (
3.49
), and (
3.47
), shows that we can apply the compactness result
[
27
, Proposition 3.1]
(with
\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
in place of
\gamma_{\delta}
, and
{U}_{m}
in place of
\Omega
) and a diagonal argument in order to extract a (non-relabeled) subsequence
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
for which the following holds:

There exists
u\in GSBD^{2}(U)
, sets of finite perimeter
E\in\mathfrak{M}(U)
,
(E_{\varepsilon}^{\ast})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\subset E_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, as well as sets (of finite perimeter)
\hat{\omega}_{u},(\hat{\omega}_{u}^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(U)
with
\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}\hat{\omega}_{u})+\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}\hat{\omega}_{u}^{\varepsilon})\leq C
such that
u\equiv 0
on
E\cup\hat{\omega}_{u}
, and as
\varepsilon\to 0
we have

(3.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm(i)}&\ \ \widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\to u\text{ in measure on $U\setminus\hat{\omega}_{u}$}\,;\\
{\rm(ii)}&\ \ \chi_{U\setminus(E^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}\cup\hat{\omega}^{\varepsilon}_{u})}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\chi_{U\setminus(E\cup\hat{\omega}_{u})}e(u)\ \ \text{weakly in $L^{2}_{\rm loc}(U;\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\rm sym})$}\,;\\
{\rm(iii)}&\ \ \mathcal{L}^{3}(\{|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}\setminus\hat{\omega}_{u})\to 0\,;\\
{\rm(iv)}&\ \ \liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\partial{E_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}}\cap U}\varphi(\nu_{E^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{surf}}^{\varphi,\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},q}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},U)\,;\\
{\rm(v)}&\ \ \lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{3}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\triangle E)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{3}(\hat{\omega}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\triangle\hat{\omega}_{u})=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{3}(E_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\setminus\hat{E}_{\varepsilon})=0\,,\end{split}

where, as in
[
27
, Equation (3.5)]
,
(\kappa_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
is such that

(3.51)

\delta_{\varepsilon}\kappa_{\varepsilon}^{3}\to 0,\ \gamma_{\varepsilon}^{3/q}\kappa_{\varepsilon}\to\infty\,\ \text{as}\ \ \varepsilon\to 0\,.

For (
3.50
)(iv), we have also used the notation

\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{surf}}^{\varphi,\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},q}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U)=\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U)+\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\cap U}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,,

see
[
27
, Equation (2.1)]
. In particular, the first two estimates in (
3.48
) imply that

(3.52)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{surf}}^{\varphi,\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},q}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\big(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\big)\,.

Let
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}
be the interpolation of
u_{\varepsilon}
being constant on cubes of sidelength
\varepsilon
in the shifted lattice
\varepsilon(\mathbb{Z}^{3}+(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2}))
(see the comments before Definition
2.7
). One can directly check that the measure convergence of
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
and
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}
are equivalent, as commented below
[
44
, Definition 2.5]
. For instance, this can be done using
[
2
, Proposition A.1 and Remark A.2]
, together with the fact that for every
i\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}
the piecewise affine interpolation
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
is constructed in such a way that

\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})=\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})=\fint_{Q_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})}\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

cf.
[
44
, Equation (6)]
. Thus, (
3.50
)(i) implies that
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\to u
in measure on
\Omega\setminus\omega_{u}
, where
\omega_{u}:=\hat{\omega}_{u}\cap\Omega
.
Moreover, by the fact
E_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
, (
3.48
)(iii), and the
L^{1}(U)
-convergence of
\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}
to
E
, we indeed obtain
E\subset\Omega
and
\chi_{Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap\Omega}\to\chi_{E}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
. This finishes the proof of the first part of the theorem. ∎

4.
The validity of the Cauchy-Born rule for discrete symmetric gradients

In view of our compactness result
[
27
, Proposition 3.1]
, in particular (
3.50
) above, the aim of this section is to show that, loosely speaking, weak
L^{2}
-convergence of the symmetric gradients of the piecewise affine interpolations of
(u_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
(outside the void set) implies the weak
L^{2}
-convergence of the discrete symmetric gradients
(\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon}))_{\varepsilon>0}
(outside a slightly modified void set) towards the same continuum limit. As already mentioned, since we believe that this result can be useful also in other discrete-to-continuum problems for elastic materials with surface discontinuities, we phrase the statement in any dimension.

For its formulation, we adopt all the notations introduced in the last subsection of the Introduction, as well as Subsection
2.1
. Moreover, as before, for a discrete map
u\colon\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
we denote by
\widetilde{u}
its piecewise affine interpolation according to a triangulation subordinate to the lattice
\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, constructed as in
[
44
, Section 3.2]
. The collection of
d
-dimensional simplices used in this construction will be denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}
, and we also define

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}:=\{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\colon\,S\subset Q\}\ \ \ \text{for all }Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}\,,

where we recall the notation in and below (
1.10
). Moreover, note that by construction all
d
-simplices in
\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}
have the same
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-measure.

Theorem 4.1

(Cauchy-Born rule)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq 2
, and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded open Lipschitz set. Consider a sequence of discrete displacements
(u_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
,
u_{\varepsilon}\colon\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
for which the following holds true. There exists
u\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega)
, a set of finite perimeter
W\in\mathfrak{M}(\Omega)
with
u\equiv 0
on
W,

as well as sets of finite perimeter
(W_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

(H.1)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(W_{\varepsilon}\triangle W)=0\,,\quad\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}W_{\varepsilon})<+\infty\,;

(H.2)

\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\to u\text{ in measure on }\Omega\setminus W\,;

(H.3)

\chi_{\Omega\setminus W_{\varepsilon}}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\chi_{\Omega\setminus W}e(u)\ \ \text{weakly in }L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym})\,.

Then, there exist sets of finite perimeter
(V_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
W_{\varepsilon}\subset V_{\varepsilon}
, such that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm(i)}&\quad\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus V_{\varepsilon})=0\text{ or }\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\setminus V_{\varepsilon})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)\ \forall\,S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}\,,\forall\,Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}\,;\\
{\rm(ii)}&\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(V_{\varepsilon}\setminus W_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=0\,,\ \sup\nolimits_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}V_{\varepsilon})<+\infty\,;\\
{\rm(iii)}&\quad\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\chi_{\Omega\setminus W}e(u)Z\ \ \text{weakly in }L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})\,,\end{split}

where
Z
is defined in (
1.6
).

The second part of the compactness result is a direct consequence of Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
2.8

(ii)
.

Having in mind (
3.48
) and (
3.50
), and the definition
\omega_{u}:=\hat{\omega}_{u}\cap\Omega
, we consider the sets

(W_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
and
W
given by

(4.3)

\displaystyle W_{\varepsilon}:=(E_{\varepsilon}^{*}\cup\hat{\omega}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\cup\hat{\omega}_{u}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cap\Omega\,,\quad W:=E\cup\omega_{u}\,.

The first condition in
(H.1)
is clearly satisfied. The second one follows by the construction of the sets. Indeed, by
\min_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\varphi>0
, (
3.50
)(iv), (
3.52
), and the fact that
\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})<+\infty
, we get
\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial E_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\cap\Omega)<+\infty
. Moreover, we have
\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}\hat{\omega}_{u}^{\varepsilon})<+\infty
, see before (
3.50
).
Conditions
(H.2)
and
(H.3)
are also satisfied because of (
3.50
)(i) and (ii), respectively. Then, we can apply Theorem
4.1
(for
d=3
) to obtain sets of finite perimeter
(V_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that the desired properties in (
2.15
) and (
2.16
) are satisfied, where we also use (
3.48
)(iv), (
3.50
)(v), and (
4.2
)(ii),(iii).
∎

The main point is that
(H.3)

implies (
4.2
)(iii), up to a modification of the void set which is negligible in the limit (see (
4.2
)(ii)). A property of this form has been established by
Schmidt
in
[
44
, Theorem 2.6]
: given discrete displacements
u_{\varepsilon}\colon\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and corresponding piecewise affine interpolations
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
such that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup u
weakly in
H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
u\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then it holds that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\nabla uZ\text{ weakly in $L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})$}\,.

The proof particularly uses integration by parts for Sobolev functions and cannot be reproduced in our setting with voids. Therefore, a delicate blow-up argument and an approximation of
GSBD^{2}
functions by Sobolev functions needs to be employed to reduce our problem to the setting in (
4.4
) for the corresponding symmetric gradients. The remainder of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.1
. The reader is invited to skip the following subsections on first reading and to proceed directly with the lower bound in Section
5
.

4.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We give an outline of the main ideas of the proof, stating the key intermediate results as preliminary lemmata. The proofs of these lemmata are postponed to Subsection
4.2
. We start by showing the existence of a weak
L^{2}
-limit for the discrete symmetric gradients.

Lemma 4.2

(Compactness for discrete symmetric gradients)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq 2
, let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded open Lipschitz set, and let
(u_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
and
(W_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
satisfy all hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
. There exist sets of finite perimeter
(V_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
W_{\varepsilon}\subset V_{\varepsilon}
satisfying (
4.2
)
(i),(ii)
, and for each bounded open Lipschitz subset
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
there exists
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}(\widetilde{\Omega},\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\in(0,1)
such that

(4.5)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})}\big\|\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\big\|_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\Omega})}<+\infty\,.

In particular, there exists
\xi\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})
such that, up to subsequence (not relabeled),

(4.6)

\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\xi\quad\text{weakly in }L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})\,.

The core idea for the proof of the lemma is that discrete gradients can be related to the gradients of the corresponding piecewise affine interpolations, in the sense that there exists an absolute constant
C_{*}>0
such that for all
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}
it holds that

(4.7)

\big|\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|_{Q}\big|^{2}\leq C_{*}\fint_{Q}|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

see
[
44
, Lemma 3.5(i)]
. (Note that at this point we are dealing with discrete displacements defined on the whole lattice
\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and hence
[
44
, Lemma 3.5(ii),(iii)]
do not appear in our setting.) As this result needs to be applied on cubes of sidelength
\varepsilon
, we need to regularize the void sets
W_{\varepsilon}
by introducing
V_{\varepsilon}
, which are, loosely speaking,
\varepsilon
-cubic approximations
of
W_{\varepsilon}
. Then, (
4.5
) follows from
(H.3)
, (
4.7
), and Korn’s inequality on each cube.

As Lemma
4.2
already shows (
4.2
)(i),(ii), it will remain to prove (
4.2
)(iii). To this end, in view of (
4.6
), we need to show that

\xi(x_{0})\equiv[\chi_{\Omega\setminus W}e(u)](x_{0})Z\quad\text{for }\mathcal{L}^{d}\text{-}\text{a.e. }x_{0}\in\Omega\,.

Since by
(H.1)
and (
4.2
)(ii) we have that
\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\to\chi_{\Omega\setminus W}
strongly in
L^{2}(\Omega)
, it suffices to identify the weak limit
\xi
for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. Lebesgue point
x_{0}\in{\Omega}\setminus W
of
u
. It is not restrictive to further suppose that the
approximate gradient
of
u
at
x_{0}
exists. In fact, recall that for any
u\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega)
, for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x_{0}\in\Omega
there exists
\nabla u(x_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0}\ \rho^{-d}\mathcal{L}^{d}\Big(\Big\{x\in B_{\rho}(x_{0})\colon\,\frac{|u(x)-u(x_{0})-\nabla u(x_{0})(x-x_{0})|}{|x-x_{0}|}>\lambda\Big\}\Big)=0\text{ for all $\lambda>0$},

see
[
12
, Corollary 5.2]
or
[
26
, Theorem 2.9]
.

We can now perform a
standard blow-up procedure
around such a point
x_{0}
. To this end, we introduce some further notation. Given a sequence
(\rho_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset(0,+\infty)
, and setting
\hat{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}>0
, consider the rescaled discrete maps
\zeta_{\varepsilon}\colon\hat{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, defined via

(4.9)

\zeta_{\varepsilon}(i):=\frac{u_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}i+x_{0,\varepsilon})-u(x_{0})}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}\quad\forall i\in\hat{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,,\

where
x_{0,\varepsilon}\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
are chosen such that, for an absolute constant
C>0
, there holds

(4.10)

|x_{0,\varepsilon}-x_{0}|\leq C\varepsilon\quad\forall\varepsilon>0\,.

The piecewise affine interpolations of
\zeta_{\varepsilon}
(again according to
[
44
, Section 3.2]
) will be denoted by
\widetilde{\zeta}_{\varepsilon}
. We mention again that we identify
\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})
and
\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})
with their piecewise constant interpolations (being constant on each cell in the shifted lattice), i.e., for every
i\in\hat{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
(using again the notation
\hat{i}:=i+\hat{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{1}{2},\dots,\tfrac{1}{2})
), we have
\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})|_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})}:=\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})(i)
, where the fact that
\varepsilon=\hat{\varepsilon}\rho_{\varepsilon}
and (
2.2
) imply that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})(i)=\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})(\rho_{\varepsilon}i+x_{0,\varepsilon})\,.

As in the notations in the Introduction, we set
V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}:=\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}
, and we define

(4.12)

\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}:=\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{\varepsilon}|_{B_{1}}\in GSBD^{2}(B_{1}),\quad\text{where \ }V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}:=\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}-x_{0,\varepsilon}).

(Here and in the following, the superscript bl indicates rescaled sets and quantities in the blow-up procedure.)
With this at hand, we have the following.

Lemma 4.3

(Blow-up)
.

For
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. point
x_{0}\in\Omega\setminus W
the following holds true. There exists a sequence
(\rho_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset(0,+\infty)
, with
\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
and
\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, so that (up to a non-relabeled subsequence),

(4.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\rm{(i)}}&\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=\xi(x_{0})\,,\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=e(u)(x_{0})\,;\\
\text{\rm{(ii)}}&\ \ \limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big(\fint_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}(x_{0})}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\fint_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}(x_{0})}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)<+\infty\,;\\
\text{\rm{(iii)}}&\quad\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\to\nabla u(x_{0})y\text{\ \ in measure on }B_{1}\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0;\\
\text{\rm{(iv)}}&\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})=0\,;\\
\text{\rm{(v)}}&\quad e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup e(u)(x_{0})\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm{sym}})\,;\\
\text{\rm{(vi)}}&\quad\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup F\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})\text{ for some }F\in L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})\,,\end{split}

where
F
satisfies
\fint_{B_{1}}F(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\xi(x_{0})
, and
\zeta_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}
are defined in (
4.9
), (
4.12
).

Properties (i)–(v) essentially follow by standard blow-up arguments for
GSBD^{2}
-functions around points where the approximate gradient exists. For convenience of the reader, we will include the main arguments in the proof below. Eventually, property (vi) essentially follows from (i) and (
4.11
) by a compactness and rescaling argument.

The remaining step is the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4

(Identification of the weak limit
F
)
.

In the setting of Lemma
4.3
, the function
F
as introduced in (
4.13
)
(vi) satisfies

(4.14)

F(y)=e(u)(x_{0})Z\ \ \text{for }\mathcal{L}^{d}\text{-a.e.\ }y\in B_{1}\,.

The key idea in the proof of the lemma is to approximate the functions
(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
by a sequence of Sobolev functions
(v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
also converging to
\nabla u(x_{0})y
. This approximation is achieved by a deep result in the theory of
GSBD^{2}
-functions, namely a
Korn inequality for functions with small jump set
. From this sequence, we define discrete maps
w_{\varepsilon}
(later denoted by
v^{\eta}_{\varepsilon}
, but simplified here for convenience) by a
sampling argument
. We also introduce the corresponding piecewise affine interpolations
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}
and we prove that
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}
are close to
\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}
,
\overline{e}(w_{\varepsilon})
are close to
\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})
, and
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}
are bounded in
H^{1}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
independently of
\varepsilon>0
. In particular,
\nabla\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\nabla u(x_{0})
weakly in
L^{2}
. We then conclude by applying (
4.4
) on the sequence of Sobolev functions
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
which allows us to deduce
\overline{\nabla}w_{\varepsilon}\to\nabla u(x_{0})Z
.

With these preliminary technical lemmata at hand, Theorem
4.1
follows.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Properties (
4.2
)(i),(ii) are established in Lemma
4.2
. In view of Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.4
, we get that
\xi(x_{0})=\fint_{B_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}F(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=e(u)(x_{0})Z
for a.e.
x_{0}\in\Omega\setminus W
. Therefore, (
4.2
)(iii) follows from (
4.6
).
∎

4.2.
Proofs of technical lemmata

In this subsection we collect the proofs of the lemmata.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

We first define the sets
(V_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
such that (
4.2
)(i),(ii) are satisfied. Loosely speaking, the sets are constructed as
\varepsilon
-cubic approximations of
(W_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
. Then we prove (
4.5
) and (
4.6
).

Step 1
.(Construction)
We start by introducing some extra notation. Recall the definition of the simplices in (
4.1
) and the comment below it. In particular,
n_{d}:=\#\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}
is a purely dimensional constant,
Q=\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}}S
, and
\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|_{S}
is constant for all
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}
and all
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Recalling also the notation introduced in (
1.10
), let us set

(4.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}&:=\big\{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,W_{\varepsilon}}\colon\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q\big)\geq\theta_{d}\varepsilon^{d-1}\big\}\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad}}:=\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,W_{\varepsilon}}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}\,;\\
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},1}&:=\big\{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad}}\colon\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon}\big)\geq(1-\beta_{d})\varepsilon^{d}\big\}\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}:=\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad}}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},1}\,,\end{split}

where
\beta_{d}:=\tfrac{1}{2n_{d}}>0
,
\theta_{d}:=(\tfrac{\beta_{d}}{2C_{d}})^{(d-1)/d}
, and
C_{d}>0
denotes the relative isoperimetric constant of the
d
-dimensional cube (see
[
3
, (3.43)]
for a version stated on balls instead of cubes). We now define

(4.16)

V_{\varepsilon}:=W_{\varepsilon}\cup\underset{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}\cup\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}{\bigcup}Q\,.

Clearly, by construction and the definition of
\beta_{d}>0
we get (
4.2
)(i) since for each
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},1}
and for each
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}
it holds that

\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\setminus V_{\varepsilon})\geq\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)-\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{d}}{n_{d}}-\beta_{d}\varepsilon^{d}=\frac{\varepsilon^{d}}{2n_{d}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)\,.

The key point of the decomposition (
4.15
) and the modification in (
4.16
) lies in the fact that

(4.17)

\int_{Q}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\int_{Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\ \text{ for every $Q\subset\subset\Omega$ with }Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V^{\rm{c}}_{\varepsilon}}\,,

and that (
4.2
)(ii) holds.

Step 2
.(Proof of (
4.17
) and (
4.2
)(ii)) Let us show (
4.17
). First,
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V^{\rm{c}}_{\varepsilon}}
implies
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},1}\cup(\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,\Omega}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,W_{\varepsilon}})
. For cubes in
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,\Omega}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,W_{\varepsilon}}
(
4.17
) is clear, and for
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},1}
we use (
4.2
)(i) and the fact that
e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})
is constant on each
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|_{S}|^{2}\leq 2\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\setminus W_{\varepsilon})|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|_{S}|^{2}=2\int_{Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

We now show (
4.2
)(ii). To this end, we first observe that by
(H.1)
we have

(4.18)

\#\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{1-d}}{\theta_{d}}\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q)\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{1-d}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-d}

for some constant
C>0
depending only on (H.1) and
d
. Regarding
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}
, we claim for all
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}
that

(4.19)

\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})\ \text{ and }\ \mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\leq C_{d}\big(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q)\big)^{\frac{d}{d-1}}\leq\tfrac{\beta_{d}}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{d}\,.

Indeed, if for some
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}
we had
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})<\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})
, we would get that

\beta_{d}\varepsilon^{d}\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{d}\,,

and by applying the relative isoperimetric inequality (cf.
[
3
, (3.43)]
) on the pair
(W_{\varepsilon},Q)
, we would obtain

(4.20)

\beta_{d}\varepsilon^{d}\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})\leq C_{d}\big(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q)\big)^{\tfrac{d}{d-1}}\leq C_{d}(\theta_{d}\varepsilon^{d-1})^{\tfrac{d}{d-1}}=\tfrac{\beta_{d}}{2}\varepsilon^{d}\,,

which would yield a contradiction. Hence,
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap W_{\varepsilon})
, and by the relative isoperimetric inequality, we also deduce the second inequality in (
4.19
).

Now, for the first assertion in (
4.2
)(ii), by
(H.1)
, (
4.18
) and (
4.19
), we can estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(V_{\varepsilon}\setminus W_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}\cup\mathcal{Q}^{\rm{bad},2}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\leq\varepsilon^{d}\#\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}+C_{d}\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}\big(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q)\big)^{\frac{d}{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon+C_{d}(\theta_{d}\varepsilon^{d-1})^{\frac{1}{d-1}}\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}W_{\varepsilon}\cap Q)\leq C\varepsilon\,,

and the claim follows. Regarding the second assertion in (
4.2
)(ii), by construction we have

\displaystyle\partial^{\ast}V_{\varepsilon}\subset\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon}\cup\bigcup_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}}\partial^{\ast}Q\cup\bigcup_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}(\partial^{\ast}Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\cup\mathcal{N}\,,

where
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\mathcal{N})=0
, see
[
40
, Theorem 16.3]
. Therefore, noting that for
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}
, due to
[
40
, Theorem 16.3]
(again up to a set of zero
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
measure), there holds
\partial^{\ast}Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon}=\partial^{\ast}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\cap\partial Q
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}V_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon})+2d\varepsilon^{d-1}\#\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{good}}+\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{\ast}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\cap\partial Q\big)\,.

In view of (
4.19
), we can apply the the geometric Lemma
4.5
stated at the end of the section for
s=\frac{1}{2}
on
P=Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon}
for
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}
. By this, and by
(H.1)
and (
4.18
) we conclude

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}V_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon})+C+C\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\rm{bad},2}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{\ast}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\cap{\rm int}(Q)\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon})+1)\leq C\,,

where we used that
\partial^{\ast}(Q\setminus W_{\varepsilon})\cap{\rm int}(Q)=\partial^{\ast}W_{\varepsilon}\cap{\rm int}(Q)
by construction.
Thus, the second assertion in (
4.2
)(ii) follows.

Step 3
.(Proof of (
4.5
)–(
4.6
)) It remains to prove (
4.5
)–(
4.6
). For this purpose, let
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be an open Lipschitz subset, and with a slight abuse of notation in this step, let us denote now
V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\widetilde{\Omega}\setminus V_{\varepsilon}
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}(\widetilde{\Omega},\Omega)\in(0,1)
be small enough so that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\bigcup_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,\widetilde{\Omega}}}Q\subset\subset\Omega

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
. For every
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}
, by the classical Korn’s inequality, there exists an affine mapping
r_{Q}(x)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=A_{Q}x
, where
A_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\mathrm{skew}}
, so that for a purely dimensional constant
C>0
it holds that

\int_{Q}|\nabla(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}-r_{Q})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

This inequality, together with the fact that
\bar{e}(r_{Q})=0
, (
4.7
) and (
4.17
), allows us to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}}\int_{Q}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}}\int_{Q}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon}-r_{Q})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{Q}|\overline{\nabla}(u_{\varepsilon}-r_{Q})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{Q}|\nabla(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}-r_{Q})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{Q}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,\widetilde{\Omega}}}\int_{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\chi_{\Omega\setminus W_{\varepsilon}}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Here, we used that the affine interpolation
\widetilde{r}_{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
coincides with
r_{Q}
. This along with
(H.3)
and (
4.21
) implies (
4.5
). Eventually, (
4.6
) follows from weak compactness and (
4.5
), along with a suitable diagonal argument.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Step 1
. We again let
V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}:=\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}
. Choose a subsequence (not relabeled) such that (
4.6
) holds. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
x_{0}\in\Omega\cap W^{0}
(cf.
[
3
, Definition 3.60]
for the definition of density points), that
x_{0}

is a Lebesgue point of both
e(u)
and
\xi
, and that the approximate gradient
\nabla u(x_{0})
exists. We claim that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(i)}&\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=\xi(x_{0})\,,\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=e(u)(x_{0})\,;\\
\text{(ii)}&\ \underset{\rho\to 0^{+}}{\liminf}\ \underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\Big(\fint_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\fint_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|{e}(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)<+\infty\,;\\
\text{(iii)}&\ u_{x_{0},\rho}(y):=\frac{u(x_{0}+\rho y)-u(x_{0})}{\rho}\to\nabla u(x_{0})y\text{\ \ in measure on }B_{1}\text{ as }\rho\to 0^{+}\,;\\
\text{(iv)}&\ \underset{\rho\to 0^{+}}{\liminf}\ \underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\ \rho^{-(d-1)}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\cap B_{2\rho}(x_{0}))=0\,.\end{split}

In fact, choose any open Lipschitz subset
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
such that
x_{0}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
. Regarding (
4.22
)(i), (
4.6
) implies that for
\rho>0
sufficiently small, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=\fint_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Since
x_{0}
is a Lebesgue point of
\xi
, we get the first property in (
4.22
)(i). The second one follows analogously, by using
(H.3)
and (
4.2
)(ii) instead of (
4.6
), by
x_{0}\in\widetilde{\Omega}\cap W^{0}
, as well as the fact that
x_{0}
is a Lebesgue point of
e(u)
. Moreover, (
4.22
)(iii) follows from the definition of the approximate gradient
\nabla u(x_{0})
, see (
4.8
), which we assumed to exist at
x_{0}
.

For (
4.22
)(ii), we notice that (
4.5
) implies that for the family of Radon measures defined by
\mu^{\rm{bulk}}_{\varepsilon}:=\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}^{d}
we have that
\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})}\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bulk}}(\widetilde{\Omega})<+\infty
. Hence, there exists a finite positive Radon measure
\mu^{\rm{bulk}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
on
\widetilde{\Omega}
such that (up to a subsequence, not relabeled)
\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bulk}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\mu^{\rm{bulk}}
in the sense of measures, as
\varepsilon\to 0
. In particular, by the Radon-Nikodym theorem we can choose
x_{0}
with the additional property that

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mu^{\rm{bulk}}(B_{2\rho}(x_{0}))}{\rho^{d}}<+\infty\,.

Moreover, since
\mu^{\rm{bulk}}
is finite, except for a countable set of
\rho\in(0,1)
, we have
\mu^{\rm{bulk}}(\partial B_{2\rho}(x_{0}))=0
. This along with weak convergence of measures shows the first part of (
4.22
)(ii). The proof of the second part is the same, by using
V_{\varepsilon}\supset W_{\varepsilon}
and
(H.3)
in place of (
4.5
).

The proof of (
4.22
)(iv) is similar: by (
4.2
)(ii), up to a subsequence (not relabeled) there exists a finite positive Radon measure
\mu^{\rm{surf}}
on
\Omega
such that
the measures
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}|_{\partial^{*}V_{\varepsilon}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\mu^{\rm{surf}}
on
\Omega
in the sense of measures, as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Notice now that for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x_{0}
we have that

(4.23)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\rho^{1-d}\mu^{\rm{surf}}(B_{2\rho}(x_{0}))=0\,,

and we can thus assume without restriction that
x_{0}
satisfies also (
4.23
). Indeed, suppose by contradiction that there exists a Borel set
C\subset\Omega
with
\mathcal{L}^{d}(C)>0
and
t>0
such that

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\rho^{1-d}\mu^{\rm{surf}}(B_{2\rho}(x))>t\quad\forall x\in C\,.

Then, as a consequence of
[
3
, Theorem 2.56]
, we would get
\mu^{\rm{surf}}(C)\geq t\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(C)
,
implying that
\mu^{\rm{surf}}(C)=+\infty
. But this is a contradiction to
\mu^{\rm{surf}}
being a finite measure. We now derive (
4.22
)(iv) from (
4.23
) by using that
\mu^{\rm{surf}}(\partial B_{2\rho}(x_{0}))=0
up to a countable number of values for
\rho
.

Step 2
.
With the properties of (
4.22
) at hand, we can now choose a suitable diagonal sequence
(\rho_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
, with
\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
and
\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, for which

(4.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(i) }&\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\ \fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=\xi(x_{0});\ \ \ \underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\ \fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x=e(u)(x_{0});\\
\text{(ii) }&\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\limsup}\Big(\fint_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\fint_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)<+\infty\,;\\
\text{(iii) }&\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)=\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}(y)\frac{\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}(x_{0,\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}y)-u(x_{0})}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}\to\nabla u(x_{0})y\text{\ \ in measure on }B_{1}\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0;\\
\text{(iv) }&\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\ \rho_{\varepsilon}^{-(d-1)}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}\cap B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0}))=0\,,\end{split}

where we recall that
V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}=\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}-x_{0,\varepsilon})
.
Indeed, recall that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\to u
in measure on
\Omega\setminus W
(see
(H.2)
) and that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(V_{\varepsilon}\triangle W)\to 0
by
(H.1)
and (
4.2
)(ii). Thus, denoting also
V_{\varepsilon,\rho}^{\rm c,bl}=\frac{1}{\rho}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}-x_{0,\varepsilon})
, (
4.22
)(iii) and the fact that
x_{0,\varepsilon}\to x_{0}
with
x_{0}\in W^{0}
, imply that for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
y\in B_{1}
it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon,\rho}^{\rm{c},bl}}(y)\frac{\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}(x_{0,\varepsilon}+\rho y)-u(x_{0})}{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\chi_{\Omega\setminus W}(x_{0}+\rho y)u_{x_{0},\rho}(y)=\nabla u(x_{0})y\,,

up to taking a subsequence in
\varepsilon>0
(not relabeled).
In combination with (
4.22
)(i), (ii), (iv), we can indeed extract a diagonal sequence
(\rho_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
with the properties claimed in (
4.24
), where we use that measure convergence is metrizable.

We now verify (
4.13
). Properties (
4.13
)(i)-(iii) are precisely (
4.24
)(i)-(iii), respectively.
Regarding (
4.13
)(iv), recall the definition of
(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
in (
4.12
). We have that

J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}=\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}((J_{\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon}))-x_{0,\varepsilon})\,.

Therefore, the fact that

\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
, we get that
|x_{0,\varepsilon}-x_{0}|\leq C\varepsilon\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough (see (
4.10
)), and we can estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-(d-1)}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon}))\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-(d-1)}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{*}V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}\cap B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})\big)\,.

Then, (
4.13
)(iv) follows from (
4.24
)(iv).

As a preparation for (
4.13
)(v),(vi), we observe that
e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})(y)=\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}(y)e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})(x_{0,\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}y)
for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
y\in B_{1}
(see (
4.12
) and argue as in (
4.11
)), and therefore, for all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that
|x_{0,\varepsilon}-x_{0}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\int_{B_{1}}\hskip-2.84544pt|e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}=\int_{B_{1}}\hskip-2.84544pt\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}(y)|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}(x_{0,\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})}\hskip-5.69046pt\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\int_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\hskip-5.69046pt\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|e(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,.

In a similar fashion, again using (
4.11
) we have

\int_{B_{1}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}|\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\int_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,.

In combination with (
4.24
)(ii), this yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(\|e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+\|\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\right)<+\infty\,.

In particular, in view of (
4.13
)(iv), we have that
