\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big(\int_{B_{1}}|e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})\Big)<+\infty\,.

Since we already know that
\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\nabla u(x_{0})y
in measure on
B_{1}
, we can infer from
[
13
, Theorem 1.1, (1.5b)]
that for a suitable subsequence we indeed have

e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup e(u)(x_{0})\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym})\,,

i.e., (
4.13
)(v) holds. In a similar fashion, we get
\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup F\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})
for some
F\in L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})
. Therefore, to see (
4.13
)(vi), it suffices to check that
\fint_{B_{1}}F(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\xi(x_{0})
. We can argue as follows. By (
4.10
) we get

\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})\triangle B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})\big)\leq C|x_{0,\varepsilon}-x_{0}|\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d-1}\leq C\varepsilon\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d-1}\,.

By this, along with the fact that
\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and (
4.13
)(ii), for
\varepsilon>0
small we can estimate

(4.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})\triangle B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\right|&\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})\triangle B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C\Big(\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})\triangle B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0}))}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{d}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\fint_{B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}|\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}}\to 0\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\end{split}

Therefore, by the fact that weak
L^{2}
-convergence implies the convergence of averages, the change of variables
x:=x_{0,\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}y
, (
4.11
), (
4.13
)(i), and (
4.25
) we obtain

\displaystyle\fint_{B_{1}}F(y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{1}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}(y)\bar{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0,\varepsilon})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}(x)\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\fint_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}(x)\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\xi(x_{0})\,.

This concludes the proof.

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

Let
x_{0}
and
(\rho_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be as in Lemma
4.3
, and recall that
\hat{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon/\rho_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. In the following, we choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
B_{2\rho_{\varepsilon_{0}}}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega
, and always suppose that
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
without further notice. In view of the blow-up properties established in Lemma
4.3
, we introduce the
blow-up
versions of our lattice, the cubic decomposition, and the
d
-dimensional simplices (cf. (
4.1
)) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}:=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{d}-x_{0,\varepsilon})\,,&\ \ \ \hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}:=\Big\{Q:Q=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(Q^{\prime}-x_{0,\varepsilon})\,;\ Q^{\prime}\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}\Big\}\,;\\
\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}:=\Big\{S:S=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(S^{\prime}-x_{0,\varepsilon})\,;\ S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\Big\}\,,&\ \ \ \mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q}^{\rm{bl}}:=\Big\{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}:S\subset Q\Big\}\ \ \forall Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\,.\end{split}

Since (
4.2
)(i) is translation and scaling invariant, we also have that

(4.26)

\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},bl})=0\text{ or }\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},bl})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)\quad\forall\,S\in\mathcal{S}^{\rm{bl}}_{\varepsilon,Q}\,,\forall\,Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\,.

In order to localize our estimates, we fix from now on a small constant
\delta\in(0,1)
, with
\delta>\widetilde{C}\hat{\varepsilon}
, which will eventually be sent to
0
. (Here
\widetilde{C}>0
is a sufficiently large dimensional constant, so that all our subsequent estimates and set inclusions are true.)

Step 1
.(Application of the Korn-Poincaré inequality in
GSBD^{2}
)
We claim that we can find an absolute constant
C>0
, sets of finite perimeter
(\omega_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{M}(B_{1})
, and Sobolev functions
(v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset H^{1}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
v_{\varepsilon}=\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}
on
B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}
, and

(4.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{H}^{d-1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\partial^{\ast}\omega_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(J_{{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})\,,\quad\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(J_{{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})^{{d/(d-1)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\,;\\
\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\leq C\left(\|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1\right)\,.\end{split}

Moreover, we can find sets of finite perimeter
({T}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(B_{1})
, with
{T}_{\varepsilon}=\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{bad}}}S
, where
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{bad}}_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
is to be specified in what follows, such that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{d}({T}_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})^{{d/(d-1)}}\,,\quad\|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1-\delta}\setminus{T}_{\varepsilon})}\leq C\left(\|e({\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1\right)\,.\end{split}

To see this, we first apply the Korn-Poincaré inequality for functions in
GSBD^{2}(B_{1})
with small jump set in the version of
[
12
, Theorem 1.2]
: there exists a dimensional constant
C>0
such that for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists a set of finite perimeter
\omega_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{1}
with

(4.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{\ast}\omega_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})\,,\quad\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})^{{d/(d-1)}}\,,

and
v_{\varepsilon}\in H^{1}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying
v_{\varepsilon}={\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}
on
B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}
and

\|e(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\leq C\|e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\,.

Now, by the classical Korn-Poincaré inequality, there exists an infinitesimal rigid motion
r_{\varepsilon}(y)=A_{\varepsilon}y+b_{\varepsilon}
,
A_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{{d}\times{d}}_{\mathrm{skew}}
,
b_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, such that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon}-r_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}(B_{1})}\leq C\|e(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\leq C\|e(\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\,.

By (
4.29
) the first part of (
4.27
) follows. Now we claim that

(4.31)

\displaystyle|A_{\varepsilon}|+|b_{\varepsilon}|\leq C\left(\|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1\right)\,.

Assuming for the moment that (
4.31
) holds, this implies
\|r_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}(B_{1})}\leq C(\|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1)
and thus, by the triangle inequality and (
4.30
), also the last inequality in (
4.27
) follows.
Let us now show (
4.31
). We define

(4.32)

K_{\varepsilon}:=D_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup(-D_{\varepsilon})\cup\omega_{\varepsilon}\cup(-\omega_{\varepsilon})\,,\quad\text{where}\quad D_{\varepsilon}:=\{y\in B_{1}\colon|{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}(y)-\nabla u(x_{0})y|>1\}\,,

and we note by (
4.13
)(iii),(iv) and (
4.29
) that

(4.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(K_{\varepsilon})\leq 2(\mathcal{L}^{d}(D_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon}))\leq C\big(\mathcal{L}^{d}(D_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})^{{d/(d-1)}}\big)\to 0\text{ \ as }\varepsilon\to 0\,.

By (
4.30
), the fact that
v_{\varepsilon}={\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}
on
B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}
, and (
4.32
) we can estimate

\displaystyle\|r_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq\|r_{\varepsilon}-{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}+\|{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla u(x_{0})\mathrm{id}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}+\|\nabla u(x_{0})\mathrm{id}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|e({\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1+|\nabla u(x_{0})|)\,,

i.e.,
\|r_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}\leq C(\|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1)
, where the last constant
C>0
depends additionally on
\nabla u(x_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Next, note that if
y\in B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon}
, then also
-y\in B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon}}y\,\mathrm{d}y=0\,.

Combining the last two observations, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\big(|A_{\varepsilon}y|^{2}+|b_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y=\|r_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{1}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})}\leq C\left(\int_{B_{1}}|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+1\right)\,.

In view of (
4.33
), (
4.31
) follows.

Next, let us construct the sets
(T_{\varepsilon}\big)_{\varepsilon>0}
. We define

(4.34)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}:=\left\{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\colon\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\cap B_{1}\cap\omega_{\varepsilon})\geq\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\cap B_{1})\right\}\,,\ {T}_{\varepsilon}:=\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}}S\cap B_{1}\,.

Let us first prove that

(4.35)

\displaystyle\|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1-\delta}\setminus T_{\varepsilon})}\leq C(\|e({\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon})\big\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1)\,.

In that respect, note that by construction, for every
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
there exists
M_{S}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{{d}\times{d}}
such that
\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}=M_{S}^{\varepsilon}
on
S\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c,bl}}
and
\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}=0
on
S\setminus V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c,bl}}
. Therefore, for every
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}
such that
S\cap B_{1-\delta}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{L}^{d}(S\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},\rm bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})>0
, we use the fact that
\delta>0
is fixed large enough with respect to
\hat{\varepsilon}
(so that
S\subset B_{1}
) and (
4.26
) to find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{S}|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y&\leq|M_{S}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S)\leq 4|M_{S}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathcal{L}^{d}((S\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},\rm bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon})=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}4\int_{S\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,.\end{split}

Summing over all
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}
such that
S\cap B_{1-\delta}\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},\rm bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\neq\emptyset
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1-\delta}\setminus{T}_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\sum_{\underset{S\cap B_{1-\delta}\cap V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c},\rm bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\neq\emptyset}{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}}}\int_{S}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{\underset{S\cap B_{1-\delta}\neq\emptyset}{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}^{\rm{bl}}}}\int_{S\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq 4\int_{B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,.

The last inequality, together with (
4.27
) and the fact that
v_{\varepsilon}={\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}
on
B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}
, yields (
4.35
). Moreover, by the definition of the sets
(T_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
, it is clear that

\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}({T}_{\varepsilon})\leq 4\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d}(J_{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}}\cap B_{1})^{d/(d-1)}\,,

which concludes the proof of (
4.28
) and of this step.

Step 2
.(Construction of a good sample for the discrete maps) In this step we define discrete mappings associated to the Sobolev functions
v_{\varepsilon}
. To this end, apart from the parameter
\delta\in(0,1)
that we fixed in the beginning of the previous step (recall that
\delta\gg\hat{\varepsilon}
), we also fix
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. Eventually, in Step 5 of the proof, we will pass to the limits
\delta,\eta\to 0
. From now on,
v_{\varepsilon}
shall denote a fixed representative of the corresponding
L^{1}
-equivalence class. In this step, we show that there exists
\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\in Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)
such that

(4.36)

\displaystyle\#\big\{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1-\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\colon\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\big\}\leq\frac{2}{(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\,,

and that for the maps
v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, defined by
v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(i):=v_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{\rm bl}_{\varepsilon}
, we have

(4.37)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\int_{B_{1}}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,,

where
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
denotes the piecewise affine interpolation of
v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
, subordinate to the lattice
\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
. To this end, let us consider the set
G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\subset Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)
defined as

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}:=\Big\{\tau\in Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)\colon\#\big\{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta})\colon\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau)\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau)\big\}\leq\frac{2}{(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\Big\}\,,

for which we claim that

(4.38)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}({G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}})\geq\frac{1}{2}(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}\,.

Indeed, consider the function
g_{\varepsilon}\colon Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)\to\mathbb{R}
, defined by

g_{\varepsilon}(\tau):=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta})}\chi_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}(i+\tau)\,.

On the one hand, since
\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}=v_{\varepsilon}
on
B_{1}\setminus\omega_{\varepsilon}
, i.e.,
\{\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\neq v_{\varepsilon}\}\subset\omega_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)}g_{\varepsilon}\geq\int_{Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)\setminus G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\#\{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta})\colon\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau)\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau)\}\,\mathrm{d}\tau>\frac{2}{(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)\setminus G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\,.

On the other hand, for
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, by exchanging summation and integration (note that the sum in the definition of the function
g_{\varepsilon}
is finite), we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)}g_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta})}\int_{Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(0)}\chi_{\omega_{\varepsilon}}(i+\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta})}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{\eta\hat{\varepsilon}}(i))\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\,.

Combining the last two inequalities, we arrive at (
4.38
).

Now, let
\tau\in G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
and
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
, say
S=\mathrm{conv}\{y_{1},\dots,y_{d+1}\}
, where
(y_{l})_{l=1,\dots,{d+1}}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
. Let
l\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. By the theory of slicing of Sobolev functions (cf.
[
3
, Proposition 3.105]
), for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
\tau\in G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
we have that
v_{\varepsilon}|_{[y_{l}+\tau,y_{l+1}+\tau]}\in H^{1}([y_{l}+\tau,y_{l+1}+\tau];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and

(4.39)

\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}v_{\varepsilon}\big(y_{l}+\tau+t(y_{l+1}-y_{l})\big)=\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\big(y_{l}+\tau+t(y_{l+1}-y_{l})\big)(y_{l+1}-y_{l})\ \ \text{ for a.e. }t\in(0,1)\,.}

Note that
|y_{l+1}-y_{l}|\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}
, that the unit vectors
\big(\frac{y_{l+1}-y_{l}}{|y_{l+1}-y_{l}|}\big)_{l=1,\dots,d}
form a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and that
|A|\leq C
for a purely dimensional constant
C>0
, where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
denotes here the corresponding transition matrix from the canonical basis
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{d}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to this basis. Let us also denote by
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\cdot\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\tau)
the piecewise affine function with
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y_{l}+\tau):=v_{\varepsilon}(y_{l}+\tau)
for
l=1,\ldots,d+1
, so that by construction,
\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}|_{S}(\cdot+\tau)
is constant on
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
. Then, using Jensen’s inequality and Fubini’s Theorem, (
4.39
), and recalling the definition in (
1.5
), we can estimate

\displaystyle\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\int_{S}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y+\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\left|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}|_{S}(\cdot+\tau)\Big(\frac{y_{l+1}-y_{l}}{|y_{l+1}-y_{l}|}\Big)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{1}{|y_{l+1}-y_{l}|^{2}}\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}|v_{\varepsilon}(y_{l+1}+\tau)-v_{\varepsilon}(y_{l}+\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\big(y_{l}+\tau+t(y_{l+1}-y_{l})\big)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\int_{(S)_{\hat{\varepsilon}}}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,.

Summing over all simplices
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
intersecting
B_{1-\delta}
(and using that
\delta\gg\hat{\varepsilon}
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y+\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\underset{S\cap B_{1-\delta}\neq\emptyset}{\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}}}\int_{S}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y+\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\underset{S\cap B_{1-\delta}\neq\emptyset}{\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}}}C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\int_{(S)_{\hat{\varepsilon}}}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\int_{B_{1}}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}|^{2}.

Here, we used that for every
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
there is only a bounded number of
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}
,
S^{\prime}\neq S
, such that
(S)_{\hat{\varepsilon}}\cap(S^{\prime})_{\hat{\varepsilon}}\neq\emptyset
. Thus, the constant
C>0
in the last inequality is purely dimensional. Using the fact that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\geq\frac{1}{2}(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}
, we can choose
\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\in G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
such that

{\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y+\tau^{\eta}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})}\int_{G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}}\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(y+\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{d}}\int_{B_{1}}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,.}

This finishes the proof of (
4.37
). As by definition of
G_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
also (
4.36
) holds, the step is concluded.

Step 3
.(Estimate of the bad lattice points)
Let
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}:=\{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\colon Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\cap B_{1-2\delta}\neq\emptyset\}
, and define the set of “good” lattice points
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}\subset\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by

(4.40)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.0pt\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}:=\big\{&i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\emptyset,\ {\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})=v_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\ \ \forall\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap[-1,1]^{d}\big\}\,.\end{split}

The other lattice points are considered to be “bad”. We claim that

(4.41)

\displaystyle\#\big(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}\big)\leq\frac{C}{\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}}\left(\eta^{-d}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{L}^{d}(T_{\varepsilon})\right)\,.

In order to prove this estimate, we note that

(4.42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}\subset\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{1}\cup\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{1}:=\{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon T_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\neq\emptyset\}\,;

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{2}:=\{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap[-1,1]^{d}\}\,.

Recall that, by its definition in (
4.34
),
T_{\varepsilon}
is a union of simplices (relative to
B_{1}
). Therefore, if
T_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathcal{L}^{d}(T_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i}))\geq c\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}
for some purely dimensional constant
c\in(0,1)
. Therefore, by (
1.12
) we obtain

(4.43)

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{1}\leq\frac{C}{\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(T_{\varepsilon}\cap Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\big)\leq\frac{C}{\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(T_{\varepsilon})\,.

On the other hand, if
i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{2}
, there exists
\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap[-1,1]^{d}
such that
\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\hat{\varepsilon}\xi+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})
. Thus, as
\delta\gg\hat{\varepsilon}
, by (
4.36
) we obtain

(4.44)

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq C\#\big\{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(B_{1-\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\colon{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\neq v_{\varepsilon}(i+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\big\}\leq\frac{C}{(\eta\hat{\varepsilon})^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega_{\varepsilon})\,.

Combining (
4.42
)–(
4.44
), we obtain (
4.41
).

Step 4
.(Weak convergence of
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
) The goal of this step is to show that, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

(4.45)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rightharpoonup\nabla u(x_{0})y\text{ weakly in }H^{1}(B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{d})\,,\ \overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\nabla u(x_{0})Z\text{ weakly in }L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})\,.

These convergence properties will be achieved by showing that

(4.46)

\displaystyle\hskip-15.00002pt\sum_{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})}\big(\hat{\varepsilon}^{-2}|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\big|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\overline{\nabla}\zeta_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\eta^{2}\,,

as well as that

(4.47)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{H^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}/2}}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \sup_{\varepsilon>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}/2}}\,.

To this end, we first prove that for each
i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}
it holds that

(4.48)

\hat{\varepsilon}^{-1}\|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})}+\|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})})}\leq C\eta\|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})})}\,.

Indeed, for
i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}
, by (
4.40
) we have
v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(j\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):=v_{\varepsilon}(j\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})={\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}(j\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})
for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}(\overline{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})})
. Therefore, for all such
j
, since
{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}
is piecewise affine in
Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(j)\subset Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})
,

\displaystyle|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(j)-{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}(j)|=|{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}(j+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})-{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}(j)|\leq|\tau_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(j)})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\eta\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})})}\,.

Thus, by the definition of the piecewise affine interpolation (cf.
[
44
, Section 3.2, Equation (6)]
) and (
4.40
), we have that for
i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}

{\hat{\varepsilon}{}^{-1}\|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq C\eta\|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}\,.

In order to finish the proof of (
4.48
), we consider a point
i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}
and a
d
-simplex
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
with
S\subset Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
, say
S=\mathrm{conv}\{x_{1},\dots,x_{d+1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\}
, where
(x_{l})_{l=1,\dots,{d+1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{bl}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Arguing as in the proof of (
4.37
) in Step 2, using that
c\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq|x_{l+1}-x_{l}|\leq C\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \text{for }l=1,\dots,d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\big|(\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon})|_{S}\big|^{2}\leq C\sum_{l=1}^{d}\frac{|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(x_{l+1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})-{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}(x_{l+1})|^{2}+|\widetilde{v}{}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(x_{l})-{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}(x_{l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})|^{2}}{\hat{\varepsilon}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C\eta^{2}\|\nabla\widetilde{z}_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}({Q_{2\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})})}\,.

This yields the desired estimate on each
S\subset Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i})
, and (
4.48
) follows.

Let us now justify (
4.46
). For this, we use the facts that
{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}
is piecewise affine (implying the equivalence between the
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
norms), (
4.48
), the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}
in (
4.40
), (
4.28
), and (
4.13
)(v), to estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})}\big({\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{-2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\eta{}^{2}\sum_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{2{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq C\eta{}^{2}\int_{B_{1-\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus{T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla{\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta{}^{2}\sup_{\varepsilon>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|e({\widetilde{z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}^{2}+1\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\eta{}^{2}\,.

The bound for the second addend in the left hand side of (
4.46
), which involves the discrete gradients, follows from the corresponding bound of the gradients of the piecewise affine interpolations. Indeed, let
Q:=Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\,,\ i\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm good}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. If
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap V^{\rm c,bl}_{\varepsilon})=0
, then
\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\overline{\nabla}\zeta_{\varepsilon}|=0
(a.e.) on
Q
. On the other hand, if
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap V^{\rm c,bl}_{\varepsilon})>0
, then (
4.26
) and an argument similar to (
4.17
) (with
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-{\widetilde{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}
instead of
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
) imply

\int_{Q}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla{\widetilde{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq C\int_{Q}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla{\widetilde{z}}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,.

Thus, by applying (
4.7
) on each cube and using the first part of (
4.46
) we arrive at the desired estimate.

Next, we verify (
4.47
). Note first that by (
4.13
)(iv), (
4.27
), (
4.28
), and (
4.41
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\Big(B_{1-2\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\setminus\bigcup_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\Big)\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\Big(\bigcup_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\Big)\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}C(\eta^{-{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\omega_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}({T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon}))=0\,.

This, together with (
4.46
) and (
4.13
)(iii), implies that
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
converges to the linear function
\nabla u(x_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})y
in measure on
B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. This yields

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{K}{}_{\varepsilon})\leq C\delta\,,\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{K}{}_{\varepsilon}:=\{y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in B_{1-\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(y)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\nabla u(x_{0})y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|>1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}\,.

Setting for brevity
b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}:=\fint_{B_{1-\delta}}\widetilde{v}^{\eta}_{\varepsilon}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y
, we can estimate

\displaystyle|b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(B_{1-\delta})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1-\delta}\setminus\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{K}{}_{\varepsilon}}|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(y)-\nabla u(x_{0})y|\,\mathrm{d}y+|\nabla u(x_{0})|\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(B_{1})+\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{K}{}_{\varepsilon}}|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 1+|\nabla u(x_{0})|+\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}(y)-b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\,\mathrm{d}y+|b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\mathcal{L}^{d}(\widetilde{K}_{\varepsilon})\,.

Therefore, by Poincaré’s and Hölder’s inequality for
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
in
B_{1-\delta}
we have

\displaystyle|b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(B_{1-\delta}\setminus\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{K}{}_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+|\nabla u(x_{0})|\big)+\|\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{1}(B_{1-\delta})}\leq C(1+\|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1-\delta})})\,,

where
C>0
depends also on
\nabla u(x_{0})
. Hence, for
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta>0
small enough, we get

|b_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\leq C(1+\|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1-\delta})})\,.

Moreover, by (
4.13
)(v), (
4.27
), and (
4.37
) we conclude

\int_{B_{1-\delta}}|\nabla\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\eta^{-d}\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\leq C\eta^{-d}(\|e(\widetilde{z\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+1){}^{2}\leq C\eta^{-d}\,.

This implies (
4.47
) for the piecewise affine interpolations
(\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})_{\varepsilon>0}
on
B_{1-\delta}
. We now apply (
4.7
) in order to obtain (
4.47
) also for
\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
on the slightly smaller ball
B_{1-2\delta}
.

Finally, we can now conclude this step by showing (
4.45
). Indeed, by (
4.47
), and having already established the measure convergence of
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}
towards
\nabla u(x_{0})y
in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we can infer that (up to a subsequence, not relabeled) it holds that
\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\nabla u(x_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
weakly in
H^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta};\mathbb{R}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. By (
4.4
) we can then also infer the weak convergence of
\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\nabla u(x_{0})Z
in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
.

Step 5
.(Weak convergence of
\chi_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c,bl}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) We are finally in the position to prove (
4.14
). To this end, let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times{2^{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
, extended by zero outside of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and write

(4.49)

\displaystyle\hskip-3.99994pt\int_{B_{1}}\hskip-3.99994pt\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-e(u)(x_{0})Z\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right):\varphi=\int_{B_{1}}\hskip-3.99994pt\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-\bar{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right):\varphi+\int_{B_{1}}\hskip-3.99994pt\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})-e(u)(x_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Z\right):\varphi\,.

Now by Step 4, recalling the notation
P
below (
1.7
) for the orthogonal projection from
\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}}
onto the space orthogonal to
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s,t}}^{d\times 2^{d}},
so that
P^{\top}=P\in\mathbb{R}^{(d\times 2^{d})\times(d\times 2^{d})}
, we have that
\overline{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})=P(\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})
and
e(u)Z=P(\nabla uZ)
. Moreover, as
x_{0}\in W^{0}
, it is not restrictive to suppose that
\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\to\chi_{B_{1}}
strongly in
L^{2}(B_{1})
by
(H.1)
and (
4.2
)(ii). Thus, by the second part of (
4.45
) it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{B_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\overline{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})-e(u)(x_{0})Z\right):\varphi\,\mathrm{d}y=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\nabla u(x_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Z\right):P\varphi\,\mathrm{d}y=0\,,

where we used that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{B_{1-2\delta}}\left(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}-\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\right):P\varphi\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1-2\delta})}\cdot\|\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c,bl}}}-\chi_{B_{1}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\to 0\,.

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{1}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\left(\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-\bar{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})\right):\varphi\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}|\overline{\nabla}\zeta_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}}(\hat{i})\cap B_{1-2\delta}}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}|\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-\overline{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})|\,.

The first integral on the right hand side can be estimated via Hölder’s inequality and (
4.46
), namely

(4.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\overline{\nabla}\zeta{}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(B_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big(\sum_{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})}\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\overline{\nabla}\zeta_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big)^{1/2}\leq C\eta\,.\end{split}

For the second integral, we can similarly use Hölder’s inequality, (
4.41
), (
4.47
), (
4.13
)(vi), and the fact that the domain of integration is nonzero only for
x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, to estimate

(4.51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}&\int_{Q_{\hat{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\hat{i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\hskip-5.69046pt\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\overline{e}(\zeta{}_{\varepsilon})-\overline{e}(v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta})|\\
&\leq C(\hat{\varepsilon}^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\#(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{good}}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{e}(\zeta{}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+\|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right)\\
&\leq C\Big(\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\omega_{\varepsilon})}}{\eta^{{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}/2}}+\sqrt{\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}({T\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\varepsilon})}\Big)\left(\|\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{e}(\zeta{}_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}+\|\overline{\nabla}v_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right)\\
&\leq C\eta^{-d}\left(\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\omega_{\varepsilon})^{1/2}+\mathcal{L}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}({T}_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{1/2}\right)\,.\end{split}

Now recalling (
4.27
), (
4.28
), and (
4.13
)(iv), (
4.49
)–(
4.51
) imply for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{1-2\delta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big|\int_{B_{1}}(\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-e(u)(x_{0})Z):\varphi\Big|\leq C\eta\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}.

Then, the desired property (
4.14
) follows by (
4.13
)(vi), by sending
\eta\to 0
, the arbitrariness of
\delta
, the boundedness of
\chi_{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c,bl\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\overline{e}(\zeta_{\varepsilon})-e(u)(x_{0})Z
in
L^{2}
, and the fact that the set
C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})
is strongly dense in
L^{2}(B_{1};\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2^{d}})
.

∎

We finish this section with the proof a geometric fact that we used in the proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
and let
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a cube. Let
P\subset Q
be a set of finite perimeter with
\mathcal{L}^{d}(P)\leq s\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
only depending on
s
and
d
such that

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q)\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P\cap{\rm int}(Q))\,.

Proof.

Without loss of generality (after possibly a rigid motion and a rescaling) we can assume that
Q
is the (open) unit cube, i.e.,
Q=(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{d}
. Thus, we suppose that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(P)\leq s
. Moreover, we can assume without restriction that

(4.52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P\cap Q)\leq\delta

for a small constant
\delta>0
depending only on
s
, to be specified along the proof. Indeed, otherwise the desired inequality clearly holds for a constant depending on
\delta
, as
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q)\leq 2d
.

We show the statement by induction over the dimension
d
. The statement for
d=2
can be deduced from
[
32
, Lemma 4.6]
as follows: suppose without restriction that
P
is connected (more precisely, indecomposable, see e.g.
[
3
, Example 4.18]
), as otherwise we argue separately for all indecomposable components. By
[
32
, Lemma 4.6]
, there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that, if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}P\cap Q)\leq\delta_{0}
, then

\mathcal{L}^{2}(P)>s\quad\text{or}\quad{\rm diam}(P)\leq C_{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}P\cap Q)\,,

for an absolute constant
C_{2}>0
, where
{\rm diam}(P):={\rm ess\,sup}\{|x-y|\colon\,x,y\in P\}
. (There, the proof has been performed for
s=\frac{1}{2}
, but it holds true for any
s\in(0,1)
, provided that
\delta_{0}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
s
.) Since
Q
is a cube, we get
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q)\leq C{\rm diam}(P)
, and since
\mathcal{L}^{2}(P)\leq s
by assumption, we deduce

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q)\leq C\,{\rm diam}(P)\leq C\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}P\cap Q).

This concludes the proof for
d=2
. Let us now assume for the inductive hypothesis that the statement holds true for some
d\geq 2
, and let us prove it also for
d+1
. We will only show that

(4.53)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q\cap\{-1/2<x_{d+1}<1/2\}\big)\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P\cap Q)\,,

where
x:=(x^{\prime},x_{d+1})\in Q
, since the surface measure in the two remaining faces of
\partial Q
can be controlled by repeating the argument after a rotation of the cube. We claim that
\mathcal{L}^{d+1}(P)\leq s
implies that for
s^{\prime}:=\frac{s+1}{2}\in(0,1)
,

(4.54)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t})\leq s^{\prime}

for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
t\in(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
, where
Q^{d}_{t}:=(-\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})^{d}\times\{t\}
. In fact, we define
\delta:=\delta(s):=s^{\prime}-s=\frac{1-s}{2}
, and suppose that (
4.52
) holds. Then, for a.e.
-\frac{1}{2}<t_{1}<t_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\big|\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t_{2}})-\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t_{1}})\big|\leq\delta\,,

since otherwise by the coarea formula we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{*}P\cap\big((-\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})^{d}\times[t_{1},t_{2}]\big)\big)\geq\big|\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t_{2}})-\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t_{1}})\big|>\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,

which contradicts our assumption (
4.52
). This observation along with
\mathcal{L}^{d+1}(P)\leq s
implies for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
t\in(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(P\cap Q^{d}_{t})\leq s+\delta=s^{\prime}.

This shows (
4.54
). By slicing properties of sets of finite perimeter we have that for a.e.
t\in(-\tfrac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
the set
P_{t}:=P\cap Q^{d}_{t}
has finite perimeter. Thus, by the inductive hypothesis, we can find a constant
C>0
depending on
d
and
s^{\prime}
such that

(4.55)

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P_{t}\cap\partial Q^{d}_{t})\leq C\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P_{t}\cap Q^{d}_{t})

for a.e.
t\in(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
. By applying the coarea formula (cf.
[
40
, (18.25)]
for
g:=\chi_{Q}
and slicing direction
e_{d+1}
), denoting by
\nu
the generalized normal to
\partial^{\ast}P
, and using (
4.55
), we can estimate

\displaystyle\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P_{t}\cap Q^{d}_{t})\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\mathrm{d}t\int_{{\partial^{\ast}P\cap\{x_{d+1}=t\}}}\chi_{Q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial^{\ast}P}\chi_{Q}\sqrt{1-(\nu\cdot e_{d+1})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d}\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(\partial^{\ast}P\cap Q)\,.

In a similar fashion, the coarea formula also implies that

\mathcal{H}^{d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\big(\partial^{*}P\cap\partial Q\cap\{-1/2<x_{d+1}<1/2\}\big)=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}P_{t}\cap\partial Q^{d}_{t})\,{\rm d}t.

These two estimates along with (
4.55
) conclude the proof of (
4.53
).
∎

5.
Lower Bound

In this section we return to the case
d=3
and focus on the proof of the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality of Theorem
2.9
. In particular, we aim at proving the following result.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
, where
E_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
and
u_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}(u_{0},\partial_{D}\Omega,E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, be such that
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\to}(u,E)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of Definition
2.7
. Then,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\geq\mathcal{F}_{0}(u,E)\,.

We split the proof of this proposition into two separate lemmata, which prove the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality for the surface part and the elastic part of the energy separately. It is not restrictive to assume that
\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})<+\infty
, since otherwise there is nothing to prove. For the next lemmata, recall the definitions in (
2.3
) and (
2.11
)–(
2.13
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
be such that
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\to}(u,E)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of Definition
2.7
. Then,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{\mathrm{per}}}(E_{\varepsilon})+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\right)\geq\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{surf}}(u,E)+\mathcal{F}^{\rm\partial_{D}}(u,E)\,.

Proof.

Let
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
be a subsequence (not relabeled) that achieves the liminf. Adopting all the notations that have been introduced so far, the desired inequality follows directly from the outcomes of our compactness result, i.e., Theorem
2.8
together with
[
27
, Remark 3.9]
. Indeed, we use
[
27
, Equation (3.28)]
along with (
2.11
) and (
2.13
), to get

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{surf}}(u,E)+\mathcal{F}^{\rm\partial_{D}}(u,E)\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{surf}}^{\varphi,\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},q}(\hat{E}_{\varepsilon},U).

This, combined with (
3.52
) concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
, where
E_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
and
u_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}(u_{0},\partial_{D}\Omega,E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, be such that
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\to}(u,E)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of Definition
2.7
. Then,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{el}}(\mathrm{id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\geq\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{el}}(u,E)\,.

Proof.

The proof is a combination of the arguments for the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality in
[
44
]
and
[
27
]
, respectively. By our compactness result, we can suppose that (
3.48
)–(
3.51
) hold, up to passing to a subsequence. We can apply Theorem
4.1
for the sets
W_{\varepsilon}
introduced in (
4.3
) to get
V_{\varepsilon}\supset W_{\varepsilon}
satisfying (
4.2
).

As a preparation, we need that (
3.50
)(iii) also holds for the discrete gradients in place of
\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
. To see this, we first observe that the fact that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
is affine on each
S\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon,Q\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
(cf. (
4.1
)) implies that there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

{\varepsilon^{3}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\#\hat{\mathcal{Q}}{}_{\varepsilon}^{\kappa_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C\sum_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\mathcal{L}^{3}(Q\cap\{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}),\quad\text{where }\hat{\mathcal{Q}}{}_{\varepsilon}^{\kappa_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:=\{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,{V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\,\|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}\,.}

For each
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, by (
4.2
)(i), the fact that
\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}
is piecewise affine, and
\omega_{u}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\omega}_{u}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset W_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset V_{\varepsilon}
for all
\varepsilon
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}(Q\cap\{|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\})\leq 2\mathcal{L}^{3}((Q\setminus V_{\varepsilon})\cap\{|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\})\leq 2\mathcal{L}^{3}((Q\setminus\hat{\omega}_{u})\cap\{|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\})\,.

By (
4.7
), there exists an absolute constant
C_{*}\geq 1
such that for all
Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\hat{Q}{}_{\varepsilon}^{\kappa_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
it holds that

\big|\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|_{Q}\big|^{2}\leq C_{*}\fint_{Q}|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C_{*}\kappa_{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq C_{*}^{2}\kappa_{\varepsilon}^{2}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Combining the previous estimates, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}(\{|\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|>C_{*}\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}\setminus V_{\varepsilon})\leq\varepsilon^{3\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\#\hat{\mathcal{Q}}{}_{\varepsilon}^{\kappa_{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\mathcal{L}^{3}(\{|\nabla\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}|>\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}\setminus\hat{\omega}_{u}).

This along with (
3.50
)(iii) shows that

(5.1)

\mathcal{L}^{3}(\{|\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|>C_{*}\kappa_{\varepsilon}\}\setminus V_{\varepsilon})\to 0\quad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}\,.

Now, let us consider an arbitrary open subset
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
, and
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}(\widetilde{\Omega},\Omega)\in(0,1)
small enough so that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
we have

(5.2)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\bigcup_{Q\in\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon,\widetilde{\Omega}\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}}Q\subset\subset\Omega,\quad\text{and \ }{\rm dist}\big((V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}},E_{\varepsilon}\big)\geq 2\varepsilon\,.}

Indeed, the second property follows from the last property in (
3.48
), the fact that
V_{\varepsilon}\supset W_{\varepsilon}\supset E_{\varepsilon}^{*}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\supset\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
4.3
) and before (
3.50
)), and that
\varepsilon/\eta_{\varepsilon}\to 0
. Thus, recalling the notation in (
1.8
), we have

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(i,E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\{1,\dots,8\}\quad\forall\ i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\big((V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}}\big)\,.

Hence, by (
2.2
) and (
2.4
), for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
we can estimate

(5.3)

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{el}}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}((V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}})}\varepsilon^{3}W^{\mathrm{el}}_{\mathrm{bulk}}\big(Z+\delta_{\varepsilon}\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}(i)\big)\,.

As a result of the minimality and regularity assumptions (ii) and (iii) in the definition of the discrete elastic cell energy (cf. Subsection
2.2
), we have that
W_{\mathrm{bulk}}^{\mathrm{el}}(Z)=0
,
DW_{\mathrm{bulk}}^{\mathrm{el}}(Z)=0
, and for every
F\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 8}
we can expand

(5.4)

W_{\mathrm{bulk}}^{\mathrm{el}}(Z+F)=\frac{1}{2}Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(F)+\Phi(F)\,,

where we recall that
Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}:=D^{2}W_{\mathrm{bulk}}^{\mathrm{el}}(Z)
and
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{3\times 8}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
|\Phi(F)|\leq C|F|^{3}
for all
F\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 8}
with
|F|\leq 1
. Again in view of assumption (iii) for the elastic cell energy, and recalling the definition of
\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})
as the projection of
\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}
on the orthogonal complement of
\mathbb{R}^{3\times 8}_{\mathrm{s,t}}
(cf. (
1.7
)), we have
Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon})=Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon}))
. Let us also define
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}=\chi_{[0,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{*}\kappa_{\varepsilon}]}(|\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}|)
with
C_{*}
from (
5.1
). Having identified
\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}
with its piecewise constant interpolation, being equal to
\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon}(i)
on each
Q_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})
, by (
5.2
), (
5.3
), (
5.4
), and the fact that
\delta_{\varepsilon}\kappa_{\varepsilon}\to 0
(see (
3.51
)), we infer that

\displaystyle\begin{split}F^{\mathrm{el}}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})&\geq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}((V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}})}\int_{Q_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\frac{1}{2}Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon}))+\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\Phi(\delta_{\varepsilon}\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{(V_{\varepsilon}^{\rm{c}})_{\rm{cub}}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\frac{1}{2}Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon}))+\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\Phi(\delta_{\varepsilon}\overline{\nabla}{u}_{\varepsilon})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}\big(\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\big)\,\mathrm{d}x+\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-2}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Phi(\delta_{\varepsilon}\overline{\nabla}u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}\big(\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}(u_{\varepsilon})\big)\,\mathrm{d}x-C\delta_{\varepsilon}\kappa_{\varepsilon}^{3}\,.\end{split}

By (
5.1
), and
\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\to\chi_{\Omega\setminus(E\cup\omega_{u})}
(cf. (
4.3
), (
3.50
)(v), and (
4.2
)(ii)), we have that
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\Omega\setminus V_{\varepsilon}}\to\chi_{\Omega\setminus(E\cup\omega_{u})}
boundedly in measure. This observation, together with (
3.51
), the Cauchy-Born rule (i.e., the weak convergence property in (
4.2
)(iii)), the convexity of
Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}
, and the fact that
u\equiv 0
on
\omega_{u}
, yields

\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}F^{\mathrm{el}}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\setminus E}Q_{\mathrm{\mathrm{bulk}}}(e(u)Z)\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Recalling the definition of the elastic energy in (
2.12
), and exhausting
\Omega
with compactly supported open subsets
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
, the statement follows.
∎

6.
Upper Bound

This final section is devoted to the proof of the
\Gamma
-limsup inequality.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
u=\chi_{\Omega\setminus E}u\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega)
and
E\in\mathfrak{M}(\Omega)
be a set of finite perimeter. Then, there exists
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
,
E_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
and
u_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}(u_{0},\partial_{D}\Omega,E_{\varepsilon})
, such that
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\to}(u,E)
in the sense of Definition
2.7
and

(6.1)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{F}_{0}(u,E)\,.

Proof.

We proceed in several steps. We first reduce to showing (
6.1
) for pairs of regular sets and displacement maps. Then, we replace these sets with polyhedral sets whose outer unit normal (at the points where this is defined) is a coordinate vector. Subsequently, we perform the approximation on the discrete level. The last step shows the convergence of the discrete energies to the respective continuum one.

Step 1
.(Density of regular sets and displacements)
By a density result, we can assume that
\partial E\cap\Omega\in C^{\infty}
and that
u\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E};\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Indeed, by
[
17
, Theorem 2.2]
, for each
E\in\mathfrak{M}(\Omega)
with
\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}E\cap\Omega)<+\infty
and each
u=\chi_{\Omega\setminus E}u\in GSBD^{2}(\Omega)
, there exists a sequence of sets
(E_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\partial E_{k}\cap\Omega\in C^{\infty}
,
\chi_{E_{k}}\to\chi_{E}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
and a sequence
(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with
u_{k}|_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{k}}}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{k}};\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
u_{k}|_{E_{k}}=0
, and
{\rm tr}(u_{k})={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{k}}
, such that
u_{k}\to u
in measure on
\Omega
and

(6.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{k}}}Q_{\rm bulk}(e(u_{k})Z)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\partial E_{k}\cap(\Omega\cup\partial_{D}\Omega)}\varphi(\nu_{E_{k}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{F}_{0}(u,E)\,.

Strictly speaking,
[
17
, Theorem 2.2]
only ensures that
\partial E_{k}
is Lipschitz and
u_{k}
is in
H^{1}
, but in the proof it is shown that
\partial E_{k}
can be chosen of class
C^{\infty}
, see
[
17
, Proposition 5.4]
, and
u_{k}
can be approximated by smooth functions by standard approximation arguments. From now on, we therefore assume that
\partial E\cap\Omega\in C^{\infty}
and that
u\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E};\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Step 2
.(Reduction to polyhedral void sets with coordinate normals) By the regularity of
E
, the continuity of
\varphi
(cf. (
2.10
)), and a covering argument, we can reduce to the case of
\partial E
being polyhedral. Indeed, up to a localization argument, we can consider a sequence
(E_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\subset E
with polyhedral boundary and, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, extend
u
to a function
u_{k}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E}_{k};\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that convergence in (
6.2
) holds. As
{\rm tr}(u)={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
and
E_{k}\subset\subset\Omega
, this can be achieved in such a way that
{\rm tr}(u_{k})={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega
. Assuming now that
E\subset\subset\Omega
has polyhedral boundary and
{\rm tr}(u)={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega
, we prove that we can further restrict to the case of
\nu_{E}\in\{\pm e_{1},\pm e_{2},\pm e_{3}\}
. To this end, we fix
\sigma>0
and define

(6.3)

\displaystyle E_{\sigma}=\bigcup_{i\in\sigma\mathbb{Z}^{3},\ Q_{\sigma}(i)\subset E}Q_{\sigma}(i)\,.

It is obvious that
\mathcal{H}^{2}
-a.e. it holds that
\nu_{E_{\sigma}}\in\{\pm e_{1},\pm e_{2},\pm e_{3}\}
. First, it is elementary to check that
E_{\sigma}\subset E
and
\chi{}_{E_{\sigma}}\to\chi{}_{E}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
as
\sigma\to 0
, since

(6.4)

\displaystyle E\setminus E_{\sigma}\subset\{x\in E\colon\,{\rm dist}(x,\partial E)\leq\sqrt{3}\sigma\}\,.

Therefore, it is clear that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\partial E_{\sigma}\subset(\partial E)_{2\sigma}\,.

We now fix an arbitrary face
S\subset\partial E
with outer normal vector
\nu
. We show that

(6.6)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\sigma\to 0}\int_{\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma}}\varphi(\nu_{E_{\sigma}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\int_{S}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=\varphi(\nu)\mathcal{H}^{2}(S)\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Without restriction we assume that
\nu_{k}\geq 0
for all
k=1,2,3
. For convenience, we define

T_{k}:=\{y\in\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma}\colon\nu_{E_{\sigma}}(y)=e_{k}\}\,

and note that by the definition of
E_{\sigma}
in (
6.3
), due to the cubes at the boundary of the face, we have

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma})\setminus(T_{1}\cup T_{2}\cup T_{3})\big)\leq C_{S}\sigma

for a constant
C_{S}>0
depending only on
S
. We claim that for all
k=1,2,3
we have

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(T_{k})\leq\nu_{k}\mathcal{H}^{2}(S)+C_{S}\sigma\,.

Let us denote by
\Pi_{k}
the orthogonal projection of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
onto the subspace orthogonal to
e_{k}
. Fix
y^{\prime}\in\Pi_{k}((S)_{2\sigma})
. Then, for
\sigma>0
sufficiently small, by the definition of
E_{\sigma}
in (
6.3
) it follows that there exists at most one
t\in\mathbb{R}
for which
y:=y^{\prime}+te_{k}
belongs to
\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma}
and is such that
\nu_{E_{\sigma}}(y)=e_{k}
. Therefore, the coarea formula implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{y\in\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma}\colon\nu_{E_{\sigma}}(y)=e_{k}\})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}(\Pi_{k}((S)_{2\sigma}))\leq\nu_{k}\mathcal{H}^{2}(S)+C_{S}\sigma\,.

This shows (
6.8
). Now, due to (
2.10
), by our assumption that
\nu_{k}\geq 0
for all
k=1,2,3
, we have that
\varphi(\nu)=\sum_{k=1}^{3}c_{k}\nu_{k}
, where
c_{k}=\sum_{\xi\in V,\xi_{k}>0}c_{\xi}
. By using (
6.7
) and (
6.8
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial E_{\sigma}\cap(S)_{2\sigma}}\varphi(\nu_{E_{\sigma}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{3}\int_{T_{k}}\varphi(e_{k})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}+C\sigma=\sum_{k=1}^{3}c_{k}\mathcal{H}^{2}(T_{k})+C\sigma\leq\varphi(\nu)\mathcal{H}^{2}(S)+C\sigma\,,

where
C>0
here depends on
\{c_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in V}
and
S
. This shows (
6.6
). Now, summing the above estimate over all faces
S
of
\partial E
, and recalling (
6.5
), we find that
\limsup_{\sigma\to 0}\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E_{\sigma})\leq\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E)
. Similarly as before, we can also extend
u
to a function
u_{\sigma}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{\sigma}};\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
{\rm tr}(u)={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega
without affecting the elastic energy as
\sigma\to 0
. Summarizing, from now on we can assume that
E\subset\subset\Omega
is a polyhedral set and
\partial E
has coordinate normals, and
u\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E};\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
{\rm tr}(u)={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega
.

Step 3
.(Construction of a recovery sequence)
We are now in the position to construct the recovery sequence. We set

(6.9)

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}:=\bigcup_{i\in\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{3}\cap E}\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))\cap\Omega\,.

We show that
\chi_{Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap\Omega}\to\chi_{E}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Note that for
\varepsilon>0
small enough (and thus also
\eta_{\varepsilon}>0
small accordingly), we have that

{(Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap\Omega)\triangle E=Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\triangle E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset(\partial E)_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{3}(\eta_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon)/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\,.}

In fact, in this case,
x\in E\setminus Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
only if
i_{x}\notin E
, where
i_{x}\in\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
is such that
x\in Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(i_{x})
. Therefore,
\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial E)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{3}(\eta_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon)/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Similarly, if
x\in Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus E
, then again
\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial E)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{3}(\eta_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon)/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Hence, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{3}((Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap\Omega)\triangle E)\leq\mathcal{L}^{3}((\partial E)_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{3}(\eta_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon)/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\leq C\eta_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial E)\,,

where
C>0
depends on
E
. Since
\eta_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, we deduce that
\chi_{Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\cap\Omega}\to\chi_{E}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
. Moreover, we define a sequence of discrete displacements
u_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}(u_{0},\partial_{D}\Omega,E_{\varepsilon})
via

{u_{\varepsilon}(i):=\fint_{Q_{\varepsilon}(i)}u(y)\,{\rm d}y\quad\text{for every \ }i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\setminus E_{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,}

u_{\varepsilon}(i)=u_{0}(i)
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(U\setminus\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, and
u_{\varepsilon}(i)=0
for every
i\in\big(\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\setminus\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\cup E_{\varepsilon}
. By the regularity of
u
we can check that the piecewise constant interpolations
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}
(being equal to
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(i)
on each cube
Q_{\varepsilon}(\hat{i})
, see (
2.2
) and the comments below) are such that
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\to u
in measure on
\Omega
. We refer to
[
44
, Lemma 4.4]
for details. Therefore,
(u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\to}(u,E)
in the sense of Definition
2.7
(with
\omega_{u}=\emptyset
in this case).

Step 4
.(Energy convergence)
The upper bound for the elastic energy, namely

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{el}}(\mathrm{id}+\delta_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega\setminus E}Q_{\mathrm{\mathrm{bulk}}}(e(u)Z)\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

follows from the compatibility of
W^{\mathrm{el}}_{\mathrm{surf}}
and
W^{\mathrm{el}}_{\mathrm{bulk}}
(cf. assumption (vi) in the definition of the discrete elastic cell energy in Subsection
2.2
) and the assumed a priori Lipschitz bound on
u
, see
[
44
, Lemma 4.4]
and the final argument in the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 2.6]
.
In view of (
2.11
)–(
2.14
), by
E\subset\subset\Omega
,
u\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega\setminus\overline{E};\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
{\rm tr}(u)={\rm tr}(u_{0})
on
\partial_{D}\Omega
, it therefore suffices to prove that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})+F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})\right)\leq\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E)\,.

We split the proof into two estimates. First, we show that

(6.11)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E)\,.

We prove this inequality locally. Let
S\subset\partial Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be a face of a cube
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)+(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2},-\frac{\varepsilon}{2},-\frac{\varepsilon}{2})
and
let
\nu\in\{\pm e_{1},\pm e_{2},\pm e_{3}\}
be the outer unit normal vector to that face. (Note that by the definition of the set
E_{\varepsilon}
in (
6.9
),
Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})
is also an
\eta_{\varepsilon}
-scale cubic set, i.e., it consists of the union of cubes of sidelength
\eta_{\varepsilon}
.) Then,

\displaystyle\int_{S}\varphi(\nu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\sum_{\xi\in V,\ \nu\cdot\xi>0}c_{\xi}\geq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i))}\quad\sum_{\xi\in V,\ \nu\cdot\xi>0}\varepsilon^{2}c_{\xi}(1-\chi_{E_{\varepsilon}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}j\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\varepsilon\xi))\,.

Then, in view of (
2.5
) and the fact that
Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\subset\subset\Omega
for
\varepsilon>0
small (see (
6.9
) and recall that
E\subset\subset\Omega
), by taking the union of all faces of
\partial Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})
, we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{per}}(E_{\varepsilon})\leq\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}))\,.

Hence, in order to obtain (
6.11
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}))\leq\mathrm{Per}_{\varphi}(E)\,.

This claim follows exactly as the proof in Step 2, by noting that for every
k=1,2,3

|\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{x\in\partial E\colon\nu_{E}(x)=\pm e_{k}\})-\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{x\in\partial Q_{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\nu_{Q_{\varepsilon}(E)}(x)=\pm e_{k}\})|\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad\varepsilon\to 0.

Therefore, (
6.11
) follows. It finally remains to prove that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})=0\,.

To this end, we first estimate the number of vertices
i\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}
such that
E_{\varepsilon}
is not flat in
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
. Recall that
\partial E
is polyhedral, and let
\Sigma\subset\partial E
be the union of edges such that for every
x\in\Sigma
the tangent space
\mathrm{Tan}_{x}\partial E
does not exist. Since
\nu_{E}\in\{\pm e_{1},\pm e_{2},\pm e_{3}\}

\mathcal{H}^{2}
-a.e., it holds that for all
i\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus(\Sigma)_{\sqrt{3}\eta_{\varepsilon}}
the set
\partial E_{\varepsilon}
is flat in
Q_{\eta_{\varepsilon}}(i)
. Moreover, for
\eta_{\varepsilon}>0
small enough, we can estimate

(6.13)

\displaystyle\#\{i\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}\colon\mathrm{dist}(i,\Sigma)\leq\sqrt{3}\eta_{\varepsilon}\}\leq\varepsilon^{-3}\mathcal{L}^{3}((\Sigma)_{\sqrt{3}\eta_{\varepsilon}})\leq C\varepsilon^{-3}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Sigma)\,.

Note that, due to (
6.9
) and the structural assumption (iii) after the definition of the discrete curvature energy in (
2.7
), we have that

(6.14)

\displaystyle W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(i,E_{\varepsilon})\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1-q}\text{ for all such }i\,.

Therefore, using also assumption (i) below (
2.7
), together with (
6.13
) and (
6.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{curv}}(E_{\varepsilon})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}(U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\varepsilon^{3}W_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{curv}}(i,E_{\varepsilon})\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{-1-q}\#\{i\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{3}\colon\mathrm{dist}(i,\Sigma)\leq\sqrt{3}\eta_{\varepsilon}\}\leq C\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{1-q}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Sigma)\,.

Due to (
2.8
),
\gamma_{\varepsilon}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{1-q}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and hence we obtain (
6.12
). Now (
6.11
) and (
6.12
) imply (
6.10
). This concludes the proof.

∎
